# Open questions & judgement calls — the operator's decision queue

Created 2026-08-13 at operator request. **Purpose:** one place for things that need the operator's
judgement, plus decisions already made so they are not re-litigated. Companions:
`docs/PLAN.md` (the plan + work queue), `docs/campaign-results.md` (results), `AGENTS.md` (rules),
`docs/handoff.md` (last session).

**Rules for this file.** An item is added the moment a judgement call is identified, not when it
becomes urgent. An item is either **OPEN** (needs the operator), **CLOSED** (with the decision and
its date), or **CLOSED-BY-MEASUREMENT** (the question dissolved because data answered it — record
the data, because "we decided" and "we measured" age differently). Nothing is deleted; a closed item
is the record that stops it being re-asked.

---

### P546 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-30 (C255 part 2; operator may overrule): **n = 20 math500 items per arm, not 40.** With thinking ON at deployed parameters an item averages several thousand tokens; for `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` and `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` 40 items per arm is ≈ 1.5–2.5 h, ≈ 9–15 h for the six arms. ### P541 — OPEN 2026-10-29 (operator: "i'd like 'push' to mean 'it passed the advertised level'. It's ok if we say that it only passed the provisional smoke test, but a push should be passing some bar. Otherwise, i think we hold the push"): **every branch push passes a named level, enforced by the pre-push hook.** Today (P448) a branch push runs only the PII / naming scans; PASS receipts are required only for stack tags and the stack's gitlinks (P539). Interim rule (in force now, by the designer): no push without its level passed and stated. Proposal: `bench.local_gate check` refuses a branch push of stack / `../mlx-vlm` / `../mlx-serve` unless the pushed tip has (1) **full** — a PASS gate receipt for that exact sha under the current policy (today's `run`); or (2) **docs** — every introduced commit touches only `docs/**` or top-level `*.md` (scans only; the gate adds nothing a docs diff can break) — or (3) **data** — the stack's `evidence` branch (already scan-only, P447) and harvest commits touching only `benchmark/results/**` produced by the harvest tooling. Each push prints its level (`[pre-push] level: full (receipt …)` / `docs-only` / `data-only`); anything else is refused naming the missing receipt. A "smoke" level is not added (no receipt kind exists for it). Build: test-first in `benchmark/bench/tests/test_local_gate.py`, four-eyes light (an independent test-writer + one cold review; it is a safety gate). Counterpoint: every code push now costs a full gate (stack ≈ 40 min, fork ≈ 7 min) before it leaves — already the convention, now enforced.

### P537 — RULED by C252, DONE 2026-10-29 (rerun on `a4cd07e0`: P369 full PASS; T = 8K valid at offset 65) (was OPEN 2026-10-29, C248 run 2): **`Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` P369 full = NOT_PASS: E1c T = 8,192 cannot exercise its per-query straddle on S′.** Run `$STACK_WORKDIR/p369/phase2/20261029-1cce312f-full-uniform-s2` (39 min): short / 1K / 32K / 64K PASS (158 rows, max TV 0; 1K after the straddle retry to seed 202); T = 8K invalid on all 5 pre-registered seeds, "no observed per-query straddle" — the generation starts at `prompt_end = threshold − 64 = 8,128`, the commit offsets near the threshold are seed-invariant (`8128, 8130, 8133, …`) and a commit lands exactly at 8,192, so no verify block has keys on both sides of it; E1c INCOMPLETE → E2 / E7 not run. No mismatch anywhere (the `c2: identical: false` is the harness's placeholder for an all-invalid cell). On the old fork (`64afc7dd`, 2026-10-20) the same cell passed after a seed retry; S′'s kernels moved the acceptance pattern. Options: (a) a fork dev-only harness change — the straddle retry also varies `prompt_end` (pre-registered offsets, e.g. threshold − 64 / − 65 / − 66) — test-first + one cold review → a new reviewed head; then this entry's smoke + full rerun on it (≈ 2.5 h GPU) while the other two run on `1cce312f`; (b) accept E1c on 4 / 5 cells and leave the 8K boundary unverified for this entry (no E2 / E7 either, since the harness skips them) — weak; (c) leave it PROVISIONAL (C247) without a full PASS until P405 R6 re-certifies its TurboQuant KV anyway. Lead rec: (a) — it is the only path to an exactness certification, and the change is small and local to the straddle retry. Counterpoint: a harness change after the reviewed head re-opens P394 review for the harness; (c) defers that cost to a point where this entry is re-measured anyway. C247's provisional ON for this entry stands (no mismatch evidence).

### P475 — OPEN 2026-10-23, revised ×2 (operator: top two per category first; the KV format is a multi-variable tradeoff with no hard bar — "i'd take a kv cache that speeds prefill by 30 % and takes extra 5 % memory"; asks for middle grounds (8-bit); reopens the `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` KV choice): **scope = `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` + `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` (B top two = C top two), each in its ideal config = MTP state + KV format, judged as a whole tradeoff table (memory, prefill, decode, quality), no pass line.** Evidence so far: affine 8-bit on `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` (C82, 2026-09-14, pre-M57 code) peaked ≈ 1 GiB HIGHER than native16 with prefill 26–34 % slower and decode ÷ 1.8–2.3 (quantized KV leaves the fused SDPA kernel); real TurboQuant 4-bit vs affine 4-bit on `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` at 256K (P428): decode 9.98 vs 6.67 tok/s, prefill 129 vs 251 tok/s, peak 25.34 vs 31.50 GB; native16 on `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` at 256K (Wave 1 M1): ≈ 395 tok/s prefill, 13.4 decode, 37.65 GB. M42's "TQ4" (2026-09-13) was in fact affine 4-bit (P405). Middle grounds in the fork: affine 8-bit; TurboQuant at integer / .5 widths (4.5, 5, 6, 8); `quantized_kv_start` (native16 up to N tokens, quantized beyond — forwarded on the cached path); split key / value widths are blocked by P407; no fp8 KV path. Lead rec: a KV matrix per pick BEFORE Wave 2 (a flip for `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` would void Waves 1–2), on the GPU while B2 / P476 are built: arms native16, TurboQuant 4, affine 8, TurboQuant 4 with `quantized_kv_start` 131,072 (TurboQuant 6 optional); 256K phase peaks + prefill / decode at 8K / 128K / 256K, ABBA, 140 W, 10-min idle; instruments — CORRECTED 2026-10-23 after the operator's question / side-agent note ("why outside the shipped stack?"): the lead's claim that the server does not report peak memory was WRONG — every response carries the worker's `peak_memory_gb` (process high-water; no reset), and the existing router-path capacity ladder `bench.run_capacity` (M42 / C82, M50-checked) records it with prefill seconds and decode tok/s per rung. So the matrix runs THROUGH the shipped path: each arm a stack commit, a fresh lean router per leg, rungs ascending (8K → 128K → 256K, so the high-water at a rung is that rung's peak), KV engagement verified from the worker's argv + its `KV cache resolved` log line; the in-process qualify only as an optional cross-check of the chosen arm's peak (known positive 37.65 / 37.97 GB). A `bench.run_capacity` smoke first (last used 2026-09-14). ≈ 3–4 h GPU per pick; the operator weighs the table; a lossy winner then gets its quality study (R6), native16 needs none. Then: `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` Wave 2 → ship call; `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` KV pick → MTP re-certification (≈ 30 h). Ranks 3–4 / shortlists deferred. Counterpoint: the router path gives one peak per rung (no prefill / decode phase split) and lifetime high-water semantics.

### P477 — OPEN 2026-10-23 (operator: "would it help if we implemented a proper fused kernel for affine8 or affine4 or tq4?"): **a fused quantized-KV attention kernel.** MLX 0.32.3's `mx.fast.scaled_dot_product_attention` has no quantized variant; the fork's affine path is two `quantized_matmul` + an explicit softmax; TurboQuant has its own Metal kernels. Bandwidth model for `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` (16 full-attention layers, 4 KV heads, head_dim 256): native16 KV at 262,144 tokens = 17.2 GB; 8-bit + group-64 scales ≈ 9.1 GB; 4-bit ≈ 4.8 GB. Decode at 256K OFF 13.4 tok/s (≈ 75 ms / token) vs ≈ 27 tok/s short-context (≈ 37 ms) → the KV read costs ≈ 38 ms ≈ 450 GB/s, i.e. decode is bandwidth-bound on KV. A fused kernel that dequantizes in registers would read ≈ 20 ms (8-bit) / ≈ 11 ms (4-bit) → ≈ 17.5 / ≈ 21 tok/s at 256K (≈ +30 % / +55 %; half that at 128K, nil at 8K), and its peak would approach the KV shrink (≈ −8 / −12 GB) if no dequantized copy materializes; prefill is compute-bound (best case ≈ parity). Estimates, not measurements. Lead rec: worth it as a Phase-2 track, affine 8-bit first (near-lossless, MLX's packed format; the same kernel covers 4-bit; TurboQuant's kernels are a separate optimization); first an upstream-MLX check, then a time-boxed decode-only prototype microbenchmark at 256K (incl. 2–3-query MTP verify blocks, TF32 / Neural Accelerator numerics) before any integration plan. It does NOT gate `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` Wave 2: a native16 + MTP ship is a real gain now; a winning kernel reopens the KV choice for both picks later (a new ON / OFF certification on the new KV). Counterpoint: if the kernel wins, Wave 2's GPU hours certify a config that is later replaced. **Addendum 2026-10-24 (operator: keep the order; "should we also revisit using the m5max's neural engines/accelerators?"):** the GPU Neural Accelerators are ALREADY active by default (lab notebook 2026-08-31: NAX quantized-matmul / GEMM kernels, 3.8× fp16 GEMM and 3.5× 4-bit `quantized_matmul` vs the forced-off control `MLX_METAL_GPU_ARCH=applegpu_g16s`). Implications for P477: (a) single-query decode is bandwidth-bound — NAX does not help it; the fused kernel's decode gain is fewer KV bytes; (b) multi-query attention (prefill chunks, MTP verify blocks) is compute-bound — a fused quantized-KV kernel must run its QK / PV products on the NAX tensor path or it will lose prefill to native16; the hand-written TurboQuant Metal kernels likely do not use NAX while the affine path's `quantized_matmul` does (one candidate mechanism for TurboQuant prefill ≈ 129 vs affine ≈ 251 tok/s, P428 — to verify); (c) every P477 prototype measurement runs with and without NAX (the known-positive control above); (d) the separate Apple Neural Engine (ANE) is not reachable from MLX / Metal (CoreML only, fixed tensor shapes) — not a fit for 256K dynamic attention; not pursued.

### P433 — RULED 2026-10-22 → C209 (was OPEN; operator decision: a newly found harness bug, own proposal per AGENTS.md; not P426): **the opencode v2 probe's pre-check proves the `noretry.js` plugin load from the CONFIG-discovery call's stderr, which does not await plugin activation — a race.** Stack suite on `c74b0fb6` (2026-10-22): 1 failed / 7130 passed / 28 skipped / 1 xfailed; the failure `bench/tests/test_m61_p202.py::test_real_cheat_then_denied_rerun[shell]` (`ServedConfigError: M50 tripwire: pre-check server log lacks loading plugin at the canonical noretry.js path`) passes 5 / 5 in isolation and in the two earlier suites. Mechanism (`benchmark/bench/provenance.py` ≈ 2040–2063): for every scaffold except `opencode-v2-web-tg1` the load proof is `r.stderr` of the config discovery call — the code's own note: "Pinned 2.0.20 integration.list awaits Plugin.awaitActivation; config.get does not" — so under load the 'loading plugin' line can be emitted after the call returns. Fail-closed (a real probe would be REFUSED, never wrongly accepted), but a flaky test and a possible false refusal of a real v2 probe under load. Proposal: test-first — the proof for every scaffold that requires the plugin comes from the `opencode api GET /api/integration --standalone` activation call (as tg1 already does); a test where the config call lacks the line but the activation call has it must pass, and one where the activation call lacks it must refuse. Lead recommendation: approve (small, harness-only, removes the only flake seen this session). Counterpoint: one extra opencode subprocess per pre-check (≈ 1 s).

### P427 — RULED 2026-10-21 → C207 (was OPEN; operator decision; P426 build B3): **the two-family blind pairwise panel (C161 / C166 / C206) for the P426 ON vs OFF comparisons.** Proposal: (1) **judges on m5max-128** — family 1: Claude Opus 5.5 via the Claude Code `blind-judge` agent (reads only the packet files, no project instructions); family 2: GPT-5.6 Sol High via `cursor-agent` for the text / code axes (A1, A2, A4, A7) and **Gemini 3.1 Pro** via `cursor-agent` for A3 (images) — probe 2026-10-21: Gemini 3.1 Pro read a two-colour test image correctly, GPT-5.6 Sol High swapped the sides. Configured per box in `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/mlx_local_stack/judges.json`, never hard-coded; every verdict records model id, client + version, prompt sha and packet sha. (2) **packets** via the existing `run_judge_pairwise --export-packets / --ingest-packets` (blind A / B, both orders, retries 0; a failed verdict is missing and counted): A2 the existing cjudge rubric; A1 / A4 a reasoning-clarity rubric over both-correct pairs; A7 a code-quality rubric over both-pass items per session (final diff + the test summary, not the whole transcript — token cost); A3 image + reference captions + both descriptions. (3) **estimator**: per item score = mean over 2 judges × 2 orders of (ON 1, tie 0.5, OFF 0); the C161 length adjustment = a logistic fit score ~ β + γ·log(len_ON / len_OFF) (the `bench/judge_length_diag.py` precedent), reported as P(ON preferred at equal length) with an item-cluster bootstrap 95 % interval (sessions as strata for A7), raw mean beside it; Holm per plan §8. (4) cost ≈ 1,150 judge calls (A1 ≈ 320, A2 160, A3 80, A4 24, A7 ≈ 560), no GPU. Build: judges.json + family map incl. the Cursor labels, a Cursor judge runner, per-axis pair exporters, the estimator; test-first, Cursor code review ≤ 3 rounds. Lead recommendation: approve as above. Counterpoint: GPT-5.6 Sol High as the second family on code is the strongest GPT but it also reviews this project's code (a reviewer-as-judge overlap); GLM 5.2 Max or Grok 4.6 would be a third family if you prefer independence over strength.

### P430 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-21 (side-agent notes ×2; B1 code rounds 1–3): **opencode request overlap vs the per-request drafter counters.** The counters are drafter-global diffs (B1 D1). Evidence: 5,637 earlier chain requests on this box (`logs/mlx_vlm.log`, 2026-10-10…16), every `Request started` at `in_flight=1`; the probe's opencode config disables the title agent and auto-compaction. Mechanism (verified in the daemon loop, Cursor B1 round 3 CLOSED D1): `cached_session` requests run inline on the model-owning daemon thread, snapshot → diff inside `_process_cached_request`, no batch step meanwhile — so their counters are EXACT under any HTTP overlap; only `continuous_batching` shares rounds. Rule (plan §2 / §6): every ON request must be `backend=cached_session` and the leg's own server-log slice reconciles exactly (B10 `log_problems`); overlaps are counted, not invalidating (replaces the earlier in_flight / 5 % rules). The A7 pilot gate carries the cached_session check.

### P429 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-21 (run-found, P394: no review round for the harness fix; the PRODUCTION side is a separate proposal): **R5 for `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` aborted after 2 / 8 legs: the TurboQuant leg 1-B process exited rc −11 AFTER writing its complete result** (`$STACK_WORKDIR/p400/perf/20261021-r5-494aea2c-qwen36`, 19:10). macOS crash report (`python3.12-2026-10-21-191003.ips`): EXC_BAD_ACCESS at 0x60 in `new_threadstate` ← `PyGILState_Ensure` ← `mlx::core::detail::CompileCache::CacheEntry::~CacheEntry` ← `dyld::ThreadLocalVariables::finalizeList` ← `pthread_exit` ← `PyThread_exit_thread` ← `take_gil`: a background Python thread exiting during interpreter finalization destroys its thread-local MLX compile cache, whose entries hold Python callables, and re-enters the finalized interpreter. A race at exit (the 8 uniform R5 legs exited 0). **Harness fix (fork `p429-leg-exit`, red `e8ac5d94`, green `d3feb734`):** a completed leg shuts logging down, flushes and `os._exit(0)`; a failing leg still raises. R5 Qwen3.6 reruns fresh on it. **Production (open, own proposal when needed):** any worker / tool process using `mx.compile` from a non-main thread can segfault at exit (unload / stop): exit-only, after responses are sent — but a −11 exit code and a crash report per stop; the P369 / R7 subprocesses have the same exposure. Candidate fixes: join / stop the generation thread before finalization, or `mx.clear_cache`-style compile-cache release on that thread before it exits; check upstream MLX for a fix.

### P405 — OPEN 2026-10-20 (operator decision; PRODUCTION configuration defect found by the P369 smoke-9 leaf census, root-caused on the live worker): **`--kv-quant-scheme turboquant` is silently ignored by the server — every registry entry with `kv_bits: 4` + `kv_quant_scheme: turboquant` is served with AFFINE 4-bit KV (`QuantizedKVCache` → `PreallocQuantizedKVCache`), not TurboQuant.** Mechanism (verified in the live worker): `mlx_vlm/server/runtime.py:72` builds the runtime config with `default_factory=RuntimeConfig.from_env` when the server package is IMPORTED — before `cli.main` sets `KV_QUANT_SCHEME` (`cli.py:804`) — so it freezes `kv_quant_scheme="uniform"`; `app.py:949` `cfg.kv_quant_scheme or get_kv_quant_scheme()` then never reaches the env (a truthy default; `kv_bits` survives only because its default is `None`). Live: env `KV_QUANT_SCHEME=turboquant`, `get_kv_quant_scheme()` = `turboquant`, `ResponseGenerator.kv_quant_scheme` = `uniform`; the cached path runs `maybe_quantize_kv_cache`'s uniform branch (`common.py:480`, `to_quantized(group_size=64, bits=4)`) while the CLI logs `scheme=turboquant`. Upstream code (upstream `0558cbee`, #1876 "live runtime settings via /v1/settings", 2026-08-16), in the fork since its upstream merge `83e2aadb` (2026-08-22). **Affected (served affine since the submodule bump after 2026-08-22):** `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` (main), `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` (main), `Qwen3.8-27B-static-mixed-4bit`, `Qwen3.8-27B-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed`, `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-mlx-uniform-4bit`, `Qwen3.8-27B-Opus-Distill-v2-mlx-uniform-4bit`; not `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` (`kv_bits: 0`). Every measurement / certification of those models since then describes affine 4-bit KV, labelled TurboQuant (memory, quality, M6d / M63 MTP certifications); measurements before 2026-08-22 ran TurboQuant. Consequence for P400: the live `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` defect was the same length-2 shortcut on the affine quantized KV path (fix A removes it for every codec; smoke 9 on the fix: E1c PASS incl. the previously failing seeds); the P400 fixtures should add the affine codec actually served. **Decisions:** (1) fix the precedence (CLI / env over a frozen default — e.g. the config built after the CLI, or a `None` default) so TurboQuant engages — that CHANGES serving numerics and memory for six models and needs their re-measurement / re-certification; or (2) align the registry with what is served (`kv_quant_scheme: uniform`) — no serving change, honest labels, TurboQuant evaluated separately later. Also audit the other `RuntimeConfig` fields with truthy defaults for the same precedence bug. Lead recommendation: (1) as its own production proposal (test-first: a CLI-launched server must report the CLI's scheme), sequenced with P400's re-pin; until then record that the six entries serve affine KV. **Smoke 11 (2026-10-20 08:01, harness `ce04439b`, `…/p369/phase2/20261020-ce04439b-smoke11-uniform-affine`): SMOKE_PASS for `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` as served today — census `ArraysCache` 48 + `PreallocQuantizedKVCache` 16 (every KV layer affine, no last-layer exemption: the uniform branch of `maybe_quantize_kv_cache`, as F3's manifest models it), the affine `quantized_scale` positive fired live, E1c both cells PASS (seed 202 after an invalid 101), E2 PASS, E7b OBSERVED; wall 1,613 s. Harness-validation only: the TurboQuant runs (plan R3) supersede it.** **Smoke 12b (2026-10-20 08:43, fork `6911b17f` = P400 + P401 + P405, `…/p369/phase2/20261020-6911b17f-smoke12b-uniform-tq`): TurboQuant ENGAGES live for `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` — worker log `KV cache resolved: kv_bits=4.0 … kv_quant_scheme='turboquant' … TQ_KV_QUANT_MODE='mse'`; census `ArraysCache` 48 + `TurboQuantKVCache` 15 + `PreallocKVCache` 1 (the TurboQuant branch's last-entry exemption), the exact F3 manifest check clean; **E1c PASS both cells and E2 PASS on TurboQuant** (P400 fix A exact on the codec now served), cross-load identical, E7b OBSERVED; `tq_norm` positive fired. SMOKE_NOT_PASS on two harness grounds: (1) the in-support positive did not fire — the latest position's retained support was ONE token (≈ 2 / 3 of live positions are), so +0.5 on its top logit changed nothing (a harness bug, fixed test-first: the latest multi-token support, else a moved support boundary; on the recorded rows it now fires); (2) projected full run 13,140 s (wall 2,190 × 6) > the 3.5 h gate (P369 v13 V13 replaces × 6 with a phase-based projection).**

### P404 — CLOSED-BY-MEASUREMENT 2026-10-20 (R4, P405 plan v4 §5; was OPEN — the pre-registered P400 §5.3 bound was exceeded on AFFINE KV): **on the codec now served (TurboQuant, P405 in BOTH arms) fix A is within the C188 bounds for both picks — and faster.** R4 ABBA (`$STACK_WORKDIR/p400/perf/20261020-r4-e12780d0-vs-64afc7dd`, arm A `e12780d0` = `p369-mtp` + P405 production, arm B `64afc7dd` = P400 + P401 + P405; `--draft-block-size 2`, 8,192-token prompt, 256 tokens, 3 warmup + 5 measured per leg, 600 s idle, 140 W every leg): `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` median decode A 23.28 vs B 39.83 tok/s (B +71 %; legs A 23.37 / 23.24, B 39.83 / 39.84 — consistent), decode-phase peak −0.04 %; `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` A 36.89 vs B 36.99 (+0.28 %), peak 0 %. Both arms served TurboQuant 4-bit (15 `TurboQuantKVCache` + 1 native + 48 `ArraysCache`), widths [1, 2] in every request. Mechanism: the old length-2 verifier shortcut on TurboQuant (the multi-query path P400 showed non-exact under TF32) is also far slower than fix A's per-query singleton path; the −3.93 % below was specific to the affine codec. No operator exception needed. Original entry: **the P400 performance probe: `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` decodes 3.93 % slower with every verify block forced to width 2.** ABBA (`$STACK_WORKDIR/p400/perf/20261020-0f972c7a/report.json`; old tree `3a92943d` vs `p400-tq-width2`; 3 warmup + 5 measured per leg; 140 W, 80 %): median decode tok/s A 41.35 (legs 41.44 / 41.31) vs B 39.73 (39.73 / 39.72) — consistent across all four legs; median decode-phase peak +0.04 %. `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed`: −0.08 % tok/s, peak 0 % (within bounds; `joint_v1` serves its blocks). Width-2 blocks were present in every request. Lead read: the probe is the worst case by construction (deployed MTP uses block 3; width-2 rounds are the one-token-budget terminal round and adaptive block-2 choices only), so the real-traffic cost is ≈ 3.9 % × the width-2 share — likely well under 1 % — and the old speed came from an inexact computation; M6d re-measures the end-to-end MTP speedup. Recommendation: accept fix A; measure the real width-2 share in `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit`'s phase-2 runs to make the estimate a number. Counterpoint: accepting a pre-registered bound miss after the fact weakens pre-registration. Alternative: an exact fast width-2 TurboQuant path (a batched decode kernel per query) — new kernel work and review.

### P402 — OPEN 2026-10-19 (latent defect in an opt-in feature; found by the P400 plan review round 2 T9, not reproduced yet): **with `EpiCacheKVCache` (KV eviction) the `qwen3_5` explicit-position slice starts at the PHYSICAL retained KV length, not the logical sequence position.** `LanguageModel.__call__` slices a full-prompt `position_ids` buffer at `cache_offset = c0.offset`; for EpiCache that is the retained length, while the true RoPE position is `rope_offset = evicted + offset` — so after an eviction a full-length slice repeats earlier positions and passes any shape check (e.g. 5,120 tokens processed with a 4,096 budget: the slice starts at 4,096 instead of 5,120). Exposure: EpiCache is opt-in (`MLX_EPICACHE_BUDGET` > 0), set nowhere in the stack registry / scripts, and stripped by the P369 worker env allowlist — no current deployment. Proposal (when EpiCache is used): slice at `rope_offset` for EpiCache caches, with a value-level test (positions after an eviction equal plain decoding's); excluded from P400's P401 hardening.

### P401 — ROOT-CAUSED 2026-10-19 (no production exposure; test fix + optional hardening proposed, operator decision on the hardening); was OPEN (pre-existing flake, found by the fork suite on `3a92943d`; zero-flakes). **Mechanism (verified):** the test drives `generate_step` on a WARM cache (offset 6) with suffix-only `input_ids` and no explicit position metadata; the embedding helper (eager and lazy alike) returns suffix-LOCAL positions `[0..6]`, and `qwen3_5` `LanguageModel.__call__` slices them at the ABSOLUTE cache offset (`position_ids[..., cache_offset : cache_offset + seq_length]`) — `[6:10]` → length 1 for a 4-token chunk, `[10:12]` / `[12:13]` → EMPTY — so RoPE reads missing / uninitialized position data whose contents follow allocator reuse, i.e. timing. Signature matched exactly: only K (RoPE'd) differs, not V; both eager and lazy vary under load (eager-vs-own-repeat 23 / 24); forcing the position arrays early (a spy's `.tolist()`) hid it; on a quiet box 0 / 11. **Fix check:** priming absolute full-prompt positions via the production helper `dispatch._prime_cached_prefix_rope_state` before the warm suffix prefill → 0 / 25 mismatches under the same load (as-is 19 / 25). **Production exposure: none found** — every production warm-cache prefill primes absolute positions first: cached-session reuse (`dispatch.py:1055`), APC (`:1112`), prompt-end canonical prefill (`_prefill_canonical_suffix`, `:1702`, docstring "Positions are ABSOLUTE (review P1)"); lazy prompt embeddings are not involved (both paths share the hazard). **Proposed:** (1) test-only: the warm-session test primes absolute positions as production does (on the P400 branch, so `p369-mtp` stays at the reviewed head); (2) production hardening (operator decision, bundle with the P400 production change): `LanguageModel.__call__` raises when the sliced `position_ids` length ≠ the chunk length instead of running RoPE on short / empty positions, so any future unprimed caller fails loudly; a deterministic red test (the unprimed warm call raises). Earlier text:  **`test_lazy_embeddings.py::TestF6AgainstEager::test_f6_warm_session_cache_with_nonzero_initial_offset` fails intermittently** (bitwise eager vs lazy raw logits, CPU device). 1 of 6 suite runs this session; 0 / 30 isolated on a quiet box; **3 / 30 under 12 CPU-burner processes; 21 / 30 under the same load with `VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS=1`** (so not Accelerate GEMM threading — load- / timing-dependent; mechanism open; only the warm-session case with a non-zero initial cache offset fails). The test file is unchanged this session. Exposure: `lazy_prompt_embeddings=on` is part of the deployed `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` tuple, so if the lazy path's numerics depend on scheduling on GPU too, same-instance controls could be affected (the phase-2 smokes' C1 has been identical every time). Next: root-cause on CPU (which op differs, eager vs lazy, under load), then a deterministic test or a production fix — its own proposal.

### P400 — VERIFIED LIVE 2026-10-20 (unbound smoke 9 on the fix, `…/p369/phase2/20261020-0f972c7a-smoke9-uniform`: `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` E1c PASS on both cells incl. the seed-a0 attempts that differed in smoke 4 — now neutral, C1 identical; E2 PASS; E7b OBSERVED; the smoke gate failed only on the KV smoke positive, the leaf being affine `PreallocQuantizedKVCache`, not TurboQuant — P405). **Correction:** the live `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` KV is AFFINE 4-bit (P405), so the live defect was the length-2 shortcut on the affine quantized SDPA path; the TurboQuant fixture showed the same class; the tests now cover both codecs (affine red on the base structurally; numerically shown live). Cursor code review round 1 CLEARED; P404 (perf) open. Earlier: BUILT 2026-10-20 on fork `p400-tq-width2` (`d4150165`: red observed in both numerics modes, fix A green 15 / 15 TF32 off + on; every quantized exactness test passes under TF32 on; Qwen4 QSA sparse-boundary regression green; M58 default-preservation guards rewritten to the new contract; plan T1 characterization: batched B > 1 MTP is refused and falls back to plain decoding — `test_f1_lockstep_b_gt_1_generate_step_refuses_mtp`, `test_f12_public_batched_entry_points_refuse_mtp_and_count` — so the left-padded multi-row TurboQuant verify path is unreachable under MTP; `test_qwen4_mtp_fusion_matches_released_equations` fails under TF32 on ALSO at the pre-fix base — a float32-reference test, TF32-sensitive by design, unrelated); Cursor code review round 1 of 3 running. Was: OPEN 2026-10-19 (operator decision; PRODUCTION defect found by the P369 GPU smoke 4, reproduced on CPU-scale fixtures; **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED 2026-10-19**: `mlx_vlm/models/qwen3_5/speculative_verifier.py:127` serves a length-2 verify block with ONE batched masked `scaled_dot_product_attention` call (blocks > 2 use the per-query singleton loop); on a TurboQuant cache that call routes into `quantized_attention` with `L = 2`, whose multi-query path differs numerically from the `L == 1` decode path; with the branch removed the fixture is exact at every block size and length (tokens and rows), `joint_v1` never reaches the branch. Fix = route length 2 through the per-query loop; production change → after the pinned full run, own proposal, red test with TQ KV, Cursor review, re-pin): **on TurboQuant 4-bit KV a width-2 MTP verify block is not exact — the uniform pick's MTP is not lossless.** Live (smoke 4, `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit`, TQ4 KV, `per_query`): in 3 of 4 E1c cells (T = 1,024 seed a0, T = 65,536 seeds a0 and a1 — a1 a VALID straddling cell) the same-instance ON vs OFF1 comparison differs at exactly the length-terminal position (row 159: same support, TV ≈ 0.009) and in the final commit's state (hash + probe), with C1 (OFF1 vs OFF2) identical and every earlier row / commit bit-equal; the terminal verifier block had width 2 (key span [1119, 1120]). `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed` (native16 KV, `joint_v1`) is exact in every cell incl. its terminal blocks. Reproduced on the tiny bf16 uniform fixture (`test_mtp_exactness._quantized_pair`, which never enabled TurboQuant KV — a coverage gap): with `kv_bits=4, kv_quant_scheme="turboquant", quantized_kv_start=0` and the scripted full-accept drafter, the LAST position differs exactly when the final round has one token of budget left (n ≡ 2 mod the block size 3: n = 14, 17, 20 …), and at n = 17 seed 0 MTP-ON EMITS A DIFFERENT TOKEN than plain decoding; with the MTP block forced to 2, most positions differ and tokens diverge; blocks 3 and 4 on TQ4 and every block size on the default KV are exact. So the defect is TurboQuant KV × verify width 2 (kernel branch not yet localised). Exposure: the daily driver is MTP-OFF (C177), so no live traffic is affected now; with MTP-ON, uniform hits it at every length-budget cut with one token left and wherever the adaptive block size picks 2. Lead recommendation: (1) uniform stays MTP-OFF; its phase-2 certification is blocked on a production fix (own proposal: localise the TQ width-2 verify branch, red test on the fixture incl. TQ KV, fix, Cursor code review, re-pin PRODUCTION, rerun uniform phase 2); (2) proceed with Fable's phase 2 now (smoke passed every Fable gate; a Fable-only full run projects ≈ 2.65 h, inside the 3.5 h budget) — `--pick` Fable smoke + full on the round-3-reviewed head; (3) add TurboQuant KV to the E1a/E1c fixtures (both picks' tuples) so the fork suite covers the deployed KV codec. Alternative: hold all of phase 2 until uniform is fixed.

### P399 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-19 (operator may overrule; found by the P369 GPU smoke 4, dev only): **E1c dropped the evidence of attempts retried for validity.** A cell whose request did not straddle its threshold (or ran < 65 tokens) is retried with the next pre-registered seed, and only the accepted attempt reached the verdict — so uniform's seed-a0 cells, which differed ON vs OFF1 with C1 identical (P400), were silently discarded, and a passing retry would have reported E1c PASS. Fix (applied, test-first, fork `fa89d19b`): every attempt records valid / neutral / C1 / problems; each cell's verdict carries its rejected attempts and `pick_outcome` counts them (C1 failure → ABORT, neutrality failure → FAIL; exact booleans); the all-seeds-invalid harness error carries the attempts.

### P398 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-19 (operator may overrule; **amended after the operator-ordered Cursor round 4** (`docs/reviews/p369-c185/round4-p398…`): the stale-row diagnosis stands, but excluding forced positions with only ON's own forced-pop proof could HIDE a real defect — MTP forcing where plain decoding would not (the OFF replay emitted ON's token regardless). Fixed (fork `f0b41d44`, reviewer's design): the OFF recorder observes plain decoding's forced pops (consumed, recorded with position, never applied) and E1c requires ON's and OFF1's schedules to be equal (else neutrality FAIL; OFF1 vs OFF2 → C1 ABORT; C2 includes it). The claim now reads: every sampled position's transformed row is equal, AND the forcing schedule equals plain decoding's, AND the full state at every commit is equal.; found by the P369 GPU smoke 3, dev only, production diff empty; changes what E1c compares → plan v9 §2.2, C185 review round 3): **E1c compared a row production never samples from.** Smoke 3, Fable T = 1,024, seed a0: ON vs same-instance OFF1 rows differed at exactly one of 160 positions (position 130, TV 1.0, disjoint supports), states bit-equal, C1 identical, emitted tokens identical. Mechanism (verified from the tokens): `max_tokens` 160 resolves the thinking budget to 128, so the budget forces `\n</think>` at 129–130; ON emitted `</think>` (248069) with ZERO probability in its own recorded row (one-hot ` epsilon`, the draft path's continuation of "5."), i.e. the token was forced, not sampled. The verifier block that emitted "5" at 128 had handed the sampler rows for 129 and 130, conditioned on drafts the forced tokens replaced; forced tokens are emitted with no sampler call, so no later round re-recorded those positions and "the last record wins" kept the stale row. Plan Q15 already excludes rows after the first rejection (unreachable history); the harness had no equivalent for a forced cut. Fix (applied, test-first on the budget-trip fixture at every block position): the ON recorder keeps each verifier block's first `n_emit` rows (wrapping `_mtp_acceptance_walk` for the base position and `_mtp_plain_emission` for the cut) and records every forced pop (`_mtp_pop_forced`); `compare.forced_positions` proves the row-less ON positions are EXACTLY the forced emissions (ON's tokens there = the forced tokens in order; one unemitted pop only at a length finish; anything else raises); those positions are excluded from the ON/OFF1 row comparison and the smoke positives and reported; states are still compared at every commit, and C1 / C2 still compare every position. Consequence for the claim: E1c covers every SAMPLED position; forced thinking-budget tokens are deterministic replacements (E2 greedy identity and the state hashes cover them). Fork `e542f2ce` red, `8d1cf115` fix.

### P397 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-19 (operator may overrule; found via the C185 review round 1 E3, dev only, production diff empty): **the E1c pair's same-instance assertion was vacuous.** `worker.py` `e1c_pair` snapshotted `dict(model=id(rg.model), policy=id(policy))` and later compared it with the same expression, where `policy` is a local read once at job start — so a replaced `rg.mtp_verify_policy` during the pair could never be detected (the model half was real). Round 1 E3 found the copy of this check in the new E2 triple; the original in E1c was outside the C185 scope. Fix (applied): both paths use `instance_identity(rg)` (target + verify policy held by reference) and `same_instance(rg, ident)` (re-reads the live attributes, `is`); red-tested helper (`test_same_instance_rereads_the_live_target_and_policy`); the E1c branch itself has no branch-level test (inline in `run_job`, exercised by the GPU smoke). Fork `a44a8070`.

### P396 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-19 (operator may overrule; found closing the P369 harness review; dev / test only, production diff empty): **the P369 harness turned a TERM-ignoring worker's stop into a harness error.** The pre-existing `test_supervise_kills_a_request_past_its_deadline_and_records_it[ignore-term]` flaked (≈ 4 / 15 runs; the two earlier green suites passed by chance). Mechanism (verified): `_stop()`'s grace loop scanned with `deadline=until` (the grace end), so an iteration starting a few ms before `until` ran `ps` with `max(0.01, left)` s — the code round 5 H33 cap — `ps` timed out, the retry had no time left and `ps_table` raised; `_stop` then SIGKILLed the group and re-raised instead of proceeding to `reap()`. Live effect: any worker that outlives SIGTERM's grace (a wedged GPU thread) would end the campaign as `HARNESS_ERROR` rather than be reaped and censored. Fix (applied): the grace-loop scan is bounded by `until + PS_MARGIN_S`, as `reap()`'s scans are; a deterministic red test models a `ps` needing ≥ 0.3 s (fork `fc2dbb7e` red, `59b32042` fix; 20 / 20 green). Recorded in the round-6 closure note.

### P393 — OPEN 2026-10-19 (operator decision; from a side-agent note, lead-verified in the harness): **E2 still compares MTP-OFF and MTP-ON greedy outputs across two separately loaded workers**, so a reload effect (`docs/metrics.md`: 55 / 142 seeded tasks differed after unload + reload, mechanism open) reads as an E2 FAIL in the smoke (no reload control) and blocks the full run; in the full run the OFF-reload control only DETECTS it (E2 → INFORMATIVE_ONLY), so E2 can never pass on a box where reloads perturb greedy output. ### P390 — RECORDED 2026-10-19 (lead finding while building the P369 phase-2 harness; a wording correction, no decision needed): **phase 1's "E1a bitwise on bf16 quantized fixtures" overstated E1a.** The E1a tests assert equal laws WITHIN `tol=1e-5` on every fixture (`test_mtp_exactness.assert_equal_laws`), and the dense E1a fixture runs at logit scale 12 (peaked distributions). Measured with the phase-2 comparator (fork `fb907c1f`): on the DENSE float32 fixture at logit scale 4, MTP verifier rows and plain singleton rows are NOT bit-identical (|Δlogprob| ≈ 1e-2, TV ≈ 1e-3; KV after the first block differs) — the float32 dense path has no exact verifier kernel and is deployed nowhere; on the bf16 affine-QUANTIZED fixtures (both picks' recipes; Fable's mixed recipe keeps some projections dense, so the bf16 dense exact kernel runs too), rows, the complete valid cache state at every commit and probe logits are BIT-IDENTICAL (3 seeds × 2 recipes). So the exactness claim is a property of the deployed kernel path; E1c measures it on the real weights (and a dense float32 linear on the real verifier path, if the kernel inventory finds one, would be a finding). E1b's token identity was always exact.

### P385 — DEFERRED to a fresh session with Cursor (C181); OPEN 2026-10-18, PRIORITY RAISED: **CONFIRMED nondeterminism, incl. NaN outputs, in MLX 0.32.3's quantized sorted-dispatch MoE prefill — even evaluated alone.** Seed-corpus measurement (fork test harness, seeds 0–63 × 10 fresh processes = 640 evaluations per cell, model forward evaluated SEPARATELY from the reference): dense (bits None) length 1 / 17 and quantized length 1 (unsorted decode) — 0 misses; **4-bit length 17 (sorted expert dispatch): 16/640 miss 1e-5, 8 distinct seeds, 5 NaN outputs, max abs error 33.7; 8-bit length 17: 24/640, 12 seeds, 3 NaN, max abs 31.1**; the failing seed set differs per process (seed 57 failed at bits 4 in one process and bits 8 in another) — a kernel-level race, not an input or fusion artefact. Fused-graph variant: seed 41 bits 8 failed 1/20 processes. The fork test now skips exactly the quantized length-17 cases with this evidence (coverage reduced until root-caused). Exposure: the B picks are dense — unaffected; quantized MoE models served by the registry (`gemma-4-26B-A4B-it-OptiQ-4bit`, `NVIDIA-Nemotron-3.5-Lightning-30B-A3B-4bit`, possibly `Ornith-1.0-35B-mlx-uniform-4bit`) can produce wrong or NaN prefill activations on a few % of multi-token prefills. Earlier text below (suspected, fused-only) is superseded. Was: **suspected nondeterminism in MLX 0.32.3's quantized sorted-dispatch MoE prefill.** The fork test `test_models.py::…test_moe_matches_reference_activation_and_routing_precision` (bits 4/8, length 17 = sorted expert dispatch) was flaky; besides unseeded inputs (fixed: every test is now seeded), with FIXED seeds and identical inputs it still missed the 1e-5 bound in ~25 % of fresh processes (10/40) when the reference and the model forward were evaluated in ONE lazy graph; evaluating them separately gave 100/100 exact (error 0.0). The test was made deterministic (seeded + separate evals, fork commit `55378b68`), which ROUTES AROUND the effect — it does not explain it. Same inputs, different results across processes points at a kernel / graph-scheduling race in `gather_qmm` sorted dispatch or buffer reuse under fusion. Exposure: the B picks are dense (Qwen3.8-27B) — not exposed; quantized MoE models in the registry (`gemma-4-26B-A4B-it-OptiQ-4bit`, `NVIDIA-Nemotron-3.5-Lightning-30B-A3B-4bit`, possibly `Ornith-1.0-35B-mlx-uniform-4bit`) may produce run-to-run varying prefill logits. Proposal: a minimal standalone repro (random quantized MoE layer, model forward alone vs fused with other ops, many fresh processes, bitwise comparison); if confirmed, measure on one real MoE pick (greedy identity across 10 processes, CPU-cheap), then report upstream to MLX and decide whether MoE results need a determinism disclosure.

A14 gains exactly the manifest addition `/runtime/invocation_nonce` and an exact argv mapping that drops `--invocation-nonce <64 hex>` (like the P379 attempt flags).

### P383 — RULED by C180 (option a); was OPEN 2026-10-18 (operator decision; found investigating the P371 test flakes): **the process guard reports ambient box processes as `orphans_unattributed`.** Mechanism (proven): the cleanup sweep lists every same-uid live process; a launchd-parented (ppid 1) process in a session other than the probe's that started during the item window and is not tracked is recorded as an unattributed orphan — macOS Spotlight's `mdworker_shared` / `MDSImporterWorker` (same uid, ppid 1) fits exactly; the client's own leaks stay in the probe session (`guard.spawn` keeps it) and are excluded from the sweep, so the sweep's orphans are either `setsid`-escaped leaks (the disclosed G2 class) or ambient processes, which the production guard cannot tell apart. Reproduced on demand: with a generator of foreign-session short-lived processes, the A14 scenarios differ from their goldens 3–4 of 4 runs (only `orphans_unattributed`); idle box 0 of 110. **Tests:** FIXED under P371 (`m64-build` `22188152`; two full bars 0 failed, the second under an ambient-process generator) (real-guard tests made hermetic through the guard's existing `scan` seam: only the test's own process family is visible; the guard's deliberate-detach tests keep the real scan; a stress regression test with the ambient generator). **Production consequence needing a decision:** tg1 records orphans and never fails on them (C147) — unchanged; but M64's exact cleanup check before an incident retry (A7 / review R5) requires an EMPTY orphan list, so a Spotlight indexer starting during a failed attempt aborts the M64 chain. Options: (a) **recommended** — A7 keeps the exact schema but orphans no longer block the retry: they are recorded in the incident (as C147 does), because the guard cannot distinguish them from ambient processes and blocking on them makes every incident a coin flip on box activity; (b) keep blocking and require Spotlight disabled for the bench out-roots (`.noindex` scratch dirs / `mdutil` exclusion) — reduces but does not remove ambient processes (any app or daemon launch); (c) classify by executable location (system / app paths = ambient) — hides a model-launched app.

### P378 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-17 (operator may overrule; P371 phase 2b findings on the real opencode 2.0.20 binary + mock provider): (1) opencode's compaction SUMMARY requests carry the full tool set (8 tools) — the plan assumed tool-less; the receipt class rules are per adapter and opencode's compaction class requires tools. (2) The `X-Bench-Attempt` header goes in `providers.mlx-local.models[model].headers` (provider `settings` is refused by the M50 destination proof) and reaches every request incl. compaction; the row `overlay_sha256` strips it so seed-overlay pairing is unchanged. (3) Summary-request counts are not recoverable from the export (tokens aggregate per compaction message): the join takes a per-class request-count range (failed compaction = exactly 2, completed = 1–2); with tools on both classes a retry and the next task request can be confused in the CLASS split, never in the task totals. (4) Tool hooks: `execute.before` carries `{tool, sessionID, agent, messageID, id, input}`, `execute.after` adds `status`, `result`; `id` = the `tool_use` part id; hooks run in file-name order, so a `toolbounds.js` rejection leaves no tool-window receipt — the watchdog's "completed tool without a start receipt aborts" rule exempts `M62_TOOL_BOUND:` errors. (5) `compaction.failed` on 2.0.20: one part-less error event `{type:"compaction.failed"}`, rc 1, export `compaction{status:failed}` + `idle{outcome:failed}`; fixture pinned; scored as `compaction_failed` only for configs that register it (M64). (6) Owed to phase 3: the receipt fence is not yet taken on abort paths (a failed attempt's tokens then come only from the gate report) — fixed in 3b with a red test.

### P367 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-17, operator agreed ("i think we should fix this"), SUPERSEDES P366 (1): a per-item union `Cargo.toml` leaks the reference's approach (e.g. `permutohedron` for alphametics) and traps modern code in old versions (`rand 0.3`, `regex 0.2`) the model cannot change. Replacement: the model-facing protected manifest stays the stub's; ONE global Rust crate allowlist, identical for every item (common crates at current versions + the exact older versions the references need), all vendored offline; `Cargo.toml` editable only in `[dependencies]` with allowlisted crates and satisfiable versions (structural TOML diff; path/git/patch/build-deps/build.rs/feature or workspace edits = tamper); `Cargo.lock` regenerated offline in the container; reference trees use `.meta/Cargo-example.toml` at freeze time only; one identical disclosure line in every Rust item's instructions lists the offline crates. `--pids-limit 2048` (P366 (2)) stands.

### P366 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-17 (operator may overrule; tg2 step 2 first freeze, 152 items, 71 min): Rust 24 / Java 45 / JavaScript 48 / C++ 25 valid. Six Rust exclusions (alphametics, decimal, grep, pig-latin, poker, robot-name) are an infrastructure gap — their references need crates declared in `.meta/Cargo-example.toml` (itertools, permutohedron, num-bigint, num-traits, thiserror, regex 0.2, counter, rand 0.3) that the materializer ignored and the image does not vendor; `cpp/bank-account` (1000-thread test) exceeds `--pids-limit 512`. Calls: (1) the protected `Cargo.toml` = union of stub and example dependencies, every such crate vendored offline (the model may use exactly the reference's crates; Cargo.toml stays protected); (2) `--pids-limit 2048`. An item whose old crate does not build on the pinned toolchain stays excluded with its reason. Kept excluded by the spec rule: `java/ledger`, `java/tree-building`, `javascript/ledger` (refactoring exercises; untouched stub passes every leaf). Image rebuild → new digest; re-freeze the affected items.

### P363 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-17 (operator may overrule; tg2 step 1 Cursor round 4, G25–G27): the tg2 grader's AUTOMATIC leftover sweep (G21) cannot be made safe cheaply — it deleted by a static label (foreign objects), could remove a live grade's volumes, and was not wired into production. ### P360 — LEAD CALL 2026-10-17 (operator may overrule; from tg2 build step 1 `c4bc2f72` on `tg2-build`): two grading-policy gaps in the tg2 spec. (a) **Agent-created build outputs:** top-level `build/`, `target/`, `node_modules/`, `.gradle/` absent from the prepared tree are stripped before the tamper check and grading (running the tests is normal use, C165 2a; the grader rebuilds from source with the image's dependency bundle, so a planted package or stale artefact cannot survive); everything else stays default-deny. (b) **Crash without a report:** a per-language wrapper prints a runner-start marker before invoking cargo / gradle / jest / the Catch2 binary; a missing or malformed report AFTER the marker is a model outcome (all leaves failed — e.g. solution code calling `process.exit`); only a missing marker (container / runtime never reached the runner) is infrastructure → transport abort. Replaces crash-text heuristics. Also recorded: C++ trees mount at `/work/<slug>` (CMake project name); Rust `grep` reference does not compile (thiserror undeclared) → freeze exclusion; Rust execute merges stderr for binary-qualified leaf ids.

### P359 — DONE 2026-10-17 (C172; test-first: `stats.paired_delta` verdicts `a_better`/`b_better`/`wash` + `directional` + `stats.verdict_label`; `compare_predictor` `PASS`/`FAIL`/`loss_not_ruled_out` lever guard at axis MDE <= 10 pp; `judge_gate` `wash`; reports, README, `main_models.yaml` comments, `docs/campaign-results.md` relabelled, regrade only, no score changed; detail and the FLAGGED lever-guard consequence — the MDE-based guard was replaced per C173 by the observed loss bound (CI lower bound >= -10 pp, exact discordance bound for zero-discordance cells); all 12 stored `compare_predictor` cells ship with bounds 0-6 pp — `benchmark/results/p359_wash/P359_AUDIT.md`). Proposal: replace equivalence certification with a three-way verdict — A better / B better (interval clears zero; drives shipping, unchanged) / **wash** (no detectable difference; always printed with the axis MDE; for picks the user decides, README says indistinguishable on that axis); 'equivalent', PASS-as-equivalence and 'certified equivalent' removed from decision code (`compare_predictor`, `judge_gate`, `paired_delta`), reports, README and `main_models.yaml` comments; stored labels re-audited into the new vocabulary (P348-style, regrade only). ### C171 — RULED 2026-10-17 (operator: "go on 1 and 2" on P358): (1) the equivalence DECISION in `stats.paired_delta` becomes an exact, valid bound — Clopper–Pearson on the two discordance counts, Bonferroni-combined (each one-sided at 1 − α/4 = 98.75 % for the repo's 95 % rule): L = CP_lo(n₊) − CP_hi(n₋), U = CP_hi(n₊) − CP_lo(n₋), equivalent iff −margin < L and U < margin; the bootstrap stays for estimates / intervals; supersedes P346's degenerate-interval patch for decisions (0 discordant establishes ±5 pp only when n is large enough: n 86 → 4.97 pp, n 100 → 4.3 pp, n 50 → 8.4 pp); test-first, Cursor-reviewed. (2) Re-audit stored verdicts as in P348 (regrade only, no reruns, no score changes); labels may move BOTH ways. (3) The exact unconditional nuisance-maximised TOST is deferred.

### G3 — OPEN 2026-10-17 (residual fail-open paths in the `ProcessGuard` read-site audit; G1 scope-closed by C170 at `g1-fix` round 5 `90eee058`, merged by operator override): four Cursor round-4 findings left unfixed, each a way a young owned process can clear cleanup — P14 `uids()` denial skips even a tracked / protected / recorded identity (its children never enter the ancestry graph); P15 status-denied obligations pass through the shared `_live_denied` set (a protected root bypasses it; a concurrent `descendants_alive()` can overwrite it); P16 `_listing_edges` forgets a one-shot ppid denial when its retry succeeds, and `_refresh_descendants` collapses an exact root's denial / exit to skip (children captured just before exit lost); P17 `process_iter()` caches `Process` objects, so after pid reuse `uids()` / `status()` read the replacement while `ppid()` raises NoSuchProcess for the original (seen as a disappearance; no confirming scan). Each needs a rare race on a same-uid process; priority low-to-medium. Real fix: structural ownership (per-item container / cgroup containment, C170 (2)), which also closes G2. Listed in the KNOWN LIMITATIONS (G3) block in `proc_guard.py`.

### P358 — OPEN 2026-10-17 (operator decision; from the P357 Cursor review Q1/Q2, branch `p357-power-sim` `benchmark/m64/reviews/p357-round1.cursor-gpt-5.6-sol-high.md`): the repo's equivalence rule is anti-conservative on sparse paired binary data. `stats.paired_delta` (95 % percentile-bootstrap CI inside ±5 pp) at the −5 pp margin, n = 86: false 'equivalent' ≈ 6.7 % raw, ≈ 5.5 % after P346 removed the zero-discordance case (driven by K = 1 discordant pair), against a nominal 2.5 % per side; the power-sim's shift-bootstrap TOST passes for K ∈ {1, 2}: P = 0.178 at p_d = |Δ| = .05. Consequence: a nondegenerate `equivalent` / PASS with very few discordant pairs can certify a real −5 pp regression. Shipping is unaffected where the rule only flips on an OFF advantage (C71/M40), but CERTIFIED / equivalent labels may overstate (like P348). Proposal: (a) replace the decision interval with an exact unconditional matched-pair risk-difference TOST (maximised over the discordance nuisance parameter), test-first, Cursor-reviewed; (b) until then, suppress positive equivalence verdicts for cells with fewer than a calibrated number of discordant pairs (label 'equivalence not established — sparse discordance'); (c) re-audit stored verdicts as in P348 (regrade only, no reruns). Recommendation: (b) now + (a) + (c), CPU only.

### P356 — OPEN 2026-10-17 (operator, on tg2 sizing: "45+ GPU [h] is waaaay too much to pay per model/harness/config tuple"): proposal. Paired MDE (80 % power, α .05) ≈ 2.8·√(p_d/n); at an assumed harder-corpus discordance p_d ≈ 0.15: n 40 → ≈ 17 pp, 100 → ≈ 11 pp, 200 → ≈ 8 pp; TOST ±5 pp equivalence needs ≈ 350 paired items (≈ 35 h per arm at ≈ 6 min/item) — unaffordable per tuple on any agentic corpus. (P356a) Agentic tier answers 'large regression or failure-mode shift' (MDE ≈ 10–15 pp), not ±5 pp equivalence; fine lossy-lever decisions (≤ 5 % OFAT) stay on the cheap single-turn axes. (P356b) Reuse one reference arm per decision epoch: a new tuple pays ONE arm (paired on the reference's items + seeds), not two. (P356c) Two tiers: SCREEN for every tuple — tg2 40 items (10 per language), k = 1, ≈ 4 h, reports per-language estimates + nonconv / tokens / failure modes (needs a k = 1 exception to the agentic k = 2 rule for screens); DECIDER only when a pick or order change is at stake — ≈ 100–120 items, pre-registered POOLED estimand across languages (per-language descriptive), k = 2, group-sequential look at 50 / 100 items with stopping → ≈ 10–20 h, a few times per campaign. (P356d) Operator to set the MDE the decider must detect. Drafted in reply; not yet in the tg2 spec. **Cursor `gpt-5.6-sol-high` round 1 (operator-requested; `benchmark/chains/c147/reviews/m65/p356-round1.cursor-gpt-5.6-sol-high.md`): AGREE WITH CHANGES S1 S2 S4–S11** — numbers were optimistic: exact McNemar MDEs (80 %) at n 100 are 11 / 12 / 16 pp for p_d .15 / .182 / .303 and n 40 has no attainable 80 % effect at .15; planning p_d = .182 (M55 same-model s1 vs s2, 24/132) and .303 between picks (40/132), not .15; ±5 pp needs 514 (formal TOST) / 630 (repo 95 %-CI rule) items at .15, ≈ 1,000+ at .303; k = 2 sessions are replication, not 2n; '50/100 looks' must be a defined futility-only design; stored-reference reuse needs a frozen prompt date + contemporaneous sentinels; a k = 1 screen is diagnostic only (no PASS/REJECT; absence of findings ≠ safety); single-turn axes do NOT certify agentic behaviour (M63: OFF `client_inactivity` ×4 vs ON, changed trajectories) — refutes P356a's premise; pooled languages only as an equal-language macro-average with a large-harm HOLD. Reviewer's plan: SCREEN 40 / k = 1 ≈ 4 GPU h, diagnostic; DECIDER 80 (20 / language) k = 2 + reload ≈ 18–19 GPU h, MDE ≈ 14 pp same-model, ≈ 18 pp between picks; a 10 pp between-pick decider is unaffordable (n 120 → ≈ 15 pp, 25–28 h).

### P347 — RULED 2026-10-16 (operator: "yes, go with your rec") — option (a): M64 is an ESTIMATION SCREEN (no PASS; estimates + intervals for every endpoint; spec §10 amended). Was: OPEN (M64 build step 2 result, operator decision): the pre-launch power simulation (`benchmark/m64/power_sim.py` → `POWER_SIM.md`; inputs: run-to-run paired discordance of the same model on the same seeds, c144-base vs P223 = 7/172 = 0.041 (corrected by P357; was quoted 11/172)) gives the spec §10 accuracy claim (TOST ±5 pp in EVERY one of the 4 language × session cells) power **0.000** for both picks at true Δ = 0; a pooled one-TOST-over-86-item-sessions alternative reached 0.30–0.34 under the biased generator (superseded; P357 round 2, per-pair differences drawn directly; numbers in `benchmark/m64/POWER_SIM.md`: pooled power at Δ = 0 0.46–0.48 at p_d .041, 0.35–0.37 at .05, ≈ 0 at p_d ≥ .18; but the pooled rule is **INVALID** — it fails calibration at the margin, false pass ≈ 0.178 = P(K ∈ {1, 2}), K ~ Binomial(86, .05), at p_d = |Δ| = .05 (exact; P358 owns the decision rule), so no pooled power figure is evidence for a usable claim); 4-cell power 0/400 at every p_d (one-sided 95 % upper bound 0.0075; the −5 pp row is infeasible at p_d .041). Mechanism: 21–22-item cells cannot bound ±5 pp (0 discordant of 22 only bounds discordance at ≈ 0.13, P346). Per spec §10 M64 is therefore an **estimation screen** (point estimates + intervals, no PASS) unless the operator approves a larger N first. Options: (a) estimation screen as specified (recommended — the tg1 corpus is saturated for these picks; M64's value is tokens/task, cut/compaction/transport incidence, subjective quality and failure modes, which the screen reports); (b) enlarge N — more sessions and/or items (e.g. adding the M55 Rust/Java/JavaScript items) — pooled-claim power would still need roughly ≥ 2–3× the item-sessions, ≈ 80–120 h GPU, and a pre-registered pooled claim instead of the 4-cell intersection.

The capability difference is part of each scaffold's treatment and is disclosed per arm. Lead reading (stated to the operator, standing unless overruled): the principle covers tools/capabilities; the benchmark's loop-hygiene controls (compaction off, client retries off, equal ceilings) stay as E2.2 says. **(2b) Loop controls (operator 2026-10-16, on P341–P344):** (i) **compaction ON in every arm** — normal use; each harness is told the exposed context (opencode carrier 262,144 checked against `/health`; Pi `models.json` `contextWindow: 262144` set explicitly — its default 128,000 would compact early; dsh verified in its adapter); every compaction event is recorded, its request's tokens count to the task, per-arm compaction incidence is reported; the opencode arm uses a compaction-on carrier (new scaffold label). (ii) **client retries OFF** (a retry only fires on a transport failure = a serving fault here; AGENTS.md: abort, never grade), **and every transport failure is COUNTED**: per arm, cause (server crash / client or server timeout / dropped stream), reported as its own "transport DNF" endpoint; the leg aborts and the chain re-runs the item after restart; the same item failing on transport twice for the same arm → strict fail, `nonconv_kind: transport_dnf` (a scaffold can itself crash the server, e.g. a role the template rejects). Pi's HTTP idle timeout (default 300 s) raised to the derived client timeout. (iii) **ceilings equal** across arms: per request thinking 81,920 / max 102,400; per task the M62 gate (stall T 81,920 tokens or N 40 requests without progress, loop K 8, hard ceiling 327,680 output tokens / 150 requests). **(2c) D1 RULED 2026-10-16 (operator: "D1 agreed"):** tools that start concurrent model sessions (opencode `subagent`, code-mode `execute`; dsh `subagent`, `subagent_fork`, `ralph`; any found in the dsh inventory) are removed in every arm — a disclosed measurement exception (one worker, one cache session, single-stream gate and accounting); likewise ask-the-user tools (opencode `question`) in this headless benchmark. **(2d) D2 RULED 2026-10-16 (operator: "D2 sounds good"; P345 option d):** one shared local search tool in every arm — the repo's SearXNG (compose `searxng`, loopback :8080, image pinned by digest in the epoch) behind a pinned SearXNG MCP server configured through each client's native MCP config (dsh only if it supports MCP, else disclosed); hosted vendor search (opencode `websearch`→Exa, dsh `web_search`→DeepSeek) removed and disclosed; fetch + shell networking stay; search calls and results are P201 evidence; SearXNG running during M64 is a recorded exception to "benchmark routers lean, no docker" (no OWUI). Operator intent: keep local SearXNG usable, incl. future local knowledge bases.

### C163 — DONE 2026-10-15 (operator: "Go on c163 for dev work"). Built test-first (`benchmark/bench/tests/test_c163_audit_context_identity.py`, 22 tests); codex `gpt-6-astra` cold review: round 1 not cleared (P1 a missing digest was an unrestricted legacy escape hatch → FROZEN INVENTORY; P2 a legacy success blocked `--retry-errors` on a digest error → the record the scorer applies decides; P3 misnamed/under-parametrized tests), round 2 cleared ("no actionable findings"; inventory edge cases fail-closed: a copied grandfathered record keeps its evidence identity, an edited one changes its hash, a missing inventory rejects every digest-less record); records `benchmark/chains/c147/reviews/c163/`. Mechanism: every new sidecar record carries `context_sha256` = sha256 of `{url, command, status}` (absent == null); it is part of `answer_key.evidence_key`, so an entry whose context changed does not match and `web_audit.audit_rows` re-audits exactly it. Legacy records (no digest) are accepted ONLY if their canonical record digest is in the frozen inventory `benchmark/bench/legacy_audit_records.json` (the 5 digest-less records of the 3 committed sidecars; `build_legacy_inventory` + a test pin exact equality); they apply to the row as it is now and are counted per model as `legacy_audits` (also totalled) — a disclosure, not a tamper-proofing claim. Any other digest-less record (omitted or null) is not an audit. P223 s1 Python legacy counts: 1 (pick 1), 2 (pick 2); both reports regenerate byte-identical (their generators print no legacy count). Full suite: 4587 passed, 2 skipped (dsh), 1 xfailed, 0 failed in 7 min 36 s on `m5max` (clean rerun; an earlier run was interleaved with a killed stale run and is not the record). Probe identity moved again (`answer_key.py`, `web_audit.py`). Original: OPEN 2026-10-15 (from the P299 codex `gpt-6-astra` cold review, finding P1; inherited from P201/M61, not introduced by P299): the P201 audit identity (`answer_key.evidence_key`: row, sample, session, tool-list kind/index, evidence path/sha256/bytes, prompt sha) omits three inputs the auditor is shown — `url`, `command` and `status` (`web_audit.audit_rows` payload). A row edit that changes those while keeping the slot and the content hash keeps the old label and is not re-audited. Rows are append-only probe output, so this is a tamper/edit scenario, not churn. Proposal: persist a digest of the complete auditor payload on each record and include it in the identity; legacy records (no digest) stay accepted as recorded, with a report note; test-first (one test per input changed independently). Needs operator go.

The testable artifact is the BF16 sibling (`nvidia/NVIDIA-Nemotron-3.5-Bolt-74B-A9B-BF16`) at MLX 4-bit mixed: community quants exist (rapid-mlx 4-bit g64 4.67 bpw 43.3 GB text-only; Duckets 4-bit g64 4.61 bpw ~40 GB with vision, patched mlx-vlm `nemotron_h_omni`; rapid-mlx 3-bit). Strong where our picks are untested: Terminal-Bench 2.1 51.0, BrowseComp 66.8, HLE 25.3, WebArena 50.9. Expected outcome for B: a fight for 3rd/4th, not a new 1st (same shape as C73 drops). Possible fit: role C research/web (vision + 256K + 9B-active decode, likely 2-3x the picks' tok/s) and the latency axis. **Lead recommendation:** do not test now. Revisit when PR #2528 merges (then: sync fork, own 4-bit mixed quant from BF16 or verify a community quant tensor-by-tensor, capacity ladder, then the C144 funnel: 22-item Python session gate before any chain; C-role judge-panel candidacy decided after that). Same with opencode, and dsh"): tool-version churn is the norm. (1) **Auditor:** `answer_key` accepts an audit only if its `auditor_version` equals the auditor CLI installed NOW (pre-C158 for codex, kept by C158 for Cursor), so any CLI update — or another box — silently returns audited cells to provisional (the three P223 Cursor audits are keyed on `2026.10.01-e373342`). Proposal: an audit is valid for the record by (declared auditor, recorded version, prompt sha, evidence identity); the installed version is provenance only and never gates acceptance; a new auditor version audits only new or retried evidence. Test-first (C158 tests pin the current gate and change). (2) **Scaffold tools:** benchmarked opencode stays sha-pinned per chain (`$STACK_WORKDIR/opencode-<v>`; the version changes model behaviour, so rows never pool across versions); a release is a planned identity boundary with a cheap re-baseline (smoke chain, C154 TTL re-check), not an exception. Same for **dsh** (DeepSeek Harness, the M35 second agentic scaffold; `@deepseek-ai/dsh` pinned `0.1.2-rc.1` in `benchmark/run_dsh_probe.py` `PINNED_DSH_VERSION`, adapter frozen, a breaking-changes developer preview): keep the pin per run, re-baseline on a deliberate bump. Operator confirmed 2026-10-14 that claude, cursor, codex, opencode and dsh all update daily. **Merge note (2026-10-15, m5max-128):** this box built the same P299 semantics independently (`06a77fec`, Cursor `gpt-5.6-sol-high` plan + code review); superseded by this version at the 2026-10-15 sync. Two extra plan-review fixes from that build are ported on top as P309: `--retry-errors` never re-audits an operator-resolved `audit_error`, and an unreadable auditor version fails the audit command before anything is appended.

### P298 — DONE 2026-10-14 inside C158 (review round 2, A4: C158 newly exposed it on codex-less boxes, so it could not wait; `answer_key.accepted_audits`) — originally OPEN (from the C158 cold review, finding A1; pre-existing since P201, affects the codex path equally): `answer_key.audit_states` treats any sidecar record with a matching key as authoritative — it does not validate the record schema (a nonempty single-line `reason`, allowed `label`) and accepts an operator resolution (`operator_label`) on an `audit_error`/`unclear` record without requiring a preceding genuine auditor record for that exact key. Proposal: validate the full record shape at load and require resolutions to follow an exact-key auditor record; test-first. Not a tamper-proofing claim (that would need authenticated receipts — out of scope). Needs operator go.

### P308 — DONE 2026-10-15 (`3d2aa8fb`; Cursor `gpt-5.6-sol-high` "cleared", no findings): the P288 chain-runner flake was a real runner race — a probe cancelled (STOP) before its first manifest writes an abort manifest (`transport_abort`) and then exits; a supervise poll in that window ran the first-manifest barrier on it → `BARRIER MISMATCH` → CHAIN ABORT rc 2 instead of the honoured STOP rc 3. The live probe has the same window (cancel during pre-manifest discovery → manifest → abort). `_barrier` now runs only while no cancel is pending; the abort manifest is still validated after exit (INCOMPLETE, archived under `stopped/`, never resumed). Repro 10/48 under 12-way load → 48/48 after; `fake_probe` `FAKE_ABORT_LINGER_S` makes the window deterministic.
