### ~~O18 (b)~~ → CLOSED (operator, 2026-08-16); see O20 above for the ruling. Original text retained below.
### O18 (b). gemma CANNOT be compared to the winners at a matched budget — it is ARITHMETICALLY impossible
Raised 2026-08-14 while executing O17, from the registry rather than from old rows:

| model | `thinking_budget` | `max_tokens` | `max_kv_cache_size` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Ornith-1.0-35B-mlx-uniform-4bit` | 81920 | 102400 | 131072 |
| `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` | 81920 | 102400 | 131072 |
| `gemma-4-31B-it-qat-6bit` | **16384** | 32768 | **49152** |
| `gemma-4-26B-A4B-it-OptiQ-4bit` | **32768** | 49152 | **65536** |

`compare` refuses any comparison differing in `thinking_budget` or `max_tokens`.

⚠️⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-08-14 — MY "ARITHMETICALLY IMPOSSIBLE" CLAIM WAS WRONG, AND THE TABLE ABOVE READS
A DIRTY FILE.** I wrote that `gemma-4-31B-it-qat-6bit`'s ceiling is `0.8 × 49152 ≈ 39,300`, less than half
the winners' budget, so matching was impossible without changing what we ship. **49152 is M5's LOCAL DIRT,
not the shipped value.** The committed registry has **196608**, whose ceiling is
`0.8 × 196608 ≈ 157,286` — comfortably ABOVE 81,920.

**Measured while merging the Nemotron entry** (`git diff -- main_models.yaml` on the worker): **all four**
model entries are locally modified, not two —

| model | committed | M5 local dirt |
|---|---|---|
| `gemma-4-31B-it-qat-6bit` | 196608 | **49152** |
| `gemma-4-26B-A4B-it-OptiQ-4bit` | 262144 | **65536** |
| `Ornith-1.0-35B-mlx-uniform-4bit` | 262144 | 131072 |
| `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` | 262144 | 131072 |

**So the queue doc's claim that "gemma's 196608 and the other 262144 entry are untouched" is FALSE**, and
I built an arithmetic argument on top of it without checking the file. Same error class as the Qwen3.8 KV
estimate: I trusted a recorded description of the config instead of reading the config.

**What is actually true:** gemma is unpairable because its **declared `thinking_budget` is 16384** (a
CONFIG CHOICE in `generation_defaults`), not because of a KV wall. At the committed cap there is headroom
to declare 81920. So the real options are:
(a) leave gemma at its shipped 16384 and accept ABSOLUTE-ONLY rows — measures what we ship, never pairs;
(b) declare 81920 for benchmark runs — pairs with the winners, but then we are no longer measuring gemma's
    deployed config, and its `conv%` is already 83% at the smaller budget so it may simply loop longer.
**Recommendation: (a), and stop describing it as impossible.** But it is now a genuine choice, not a wall.

**Still queued against that constraint: ~34 h** — `gemma-4-26B-A4B-it-OptiQ-4bit` evalplus n=100,
`ifeval n=200 gemma-4-31B-it-qat-6bit` (~10 h). Plus two large winner jobs whose own names flag them:
`math500 n=100 both winners` (~35 h) and `livecodebench n=100 ×4` (~55 h, "LIKELY TOO EXPENSIVE AS
SPECIFIED").

**The counter-argument, which I think is right and which cuts against a blanket drop:** goal A is to
MEASURE models on dimensions, and a gemma row at gemma's own deployed config measures gemma **as it would
actually be used**. That is legitimate, useful data — it just yields an ABSOLUTE capability number and a
usability verdict, never a paired delta. The refusal is a property of paired statistics, not of the
measurement.

**Options:** (a) **keep the gemma jobs, labelled ABSOLUTE-ONLY** — report them in the scoresheet, never
pair them, and size n from a 5-item pilot; (b) **drop gemma from the coding axis** and spend the ~34 h on
the axes that can resolve something (BFCL, judge-panel reliability, an opencode row for the B pick);
(c) re-run the winners at gemma's budget — rejected, it would misrepresent the winners and cost more.
**Recommendation: (a) for `ifeval n=200`, (b) for `gemma-4-26B-A4B-it-OptiQ-4bit evalplus n=100`.** The
daily-driver dimension has only two models and an absolute third is genuinely informative; the coding
dimension already has an unresolved 2–5pp question between the winners that a ±20pp uncomparable row
cannot help with. **Needs your ruling; nothing is dropped beyond the one job you already approved.**

### ~~O17 (original text, retained)~~ `gemma-4-31B-it-qat-6bit` evalplus is queued at n=40 — but it is UNRANKABLE by construction
The last queued job is `gemma-4-31B-it-qat-6bit evalplus n=40`, deferred earlier today with the note
"10.4h projected for a ±20pp row, 83% of wall in runaways, 1 item lost to a 3600s timeout".

**Two problems, and the second is the decisive one:**
1. **±20pp at n=40** cannot resolve anything the campaign cares about. The winners differ by ~2–5pp on
   coding; resolving that needs 628 matched items.
2. **It runs at `thinking_budget` 16384 while both winners sit at 81920**, and `compare` MECHANICALLY
   REFUSES cross-budget comparisons. So 10.4 h of the single worker buys a row that cannot legally be
   compared to anything already measured. It would sit in the scoresheet as an unrankable cell.

**Options:** (a) **drop it** — 10.4 h back for work that can resolve something; (b) **re-run it at a
matched 81920 budget**, making it comparable but costing more than 10.4 h and re-opening whether gemma
converges at that budget (its `conv%` is already 83% on humanevalplus at 16384); (c) run as-is and
accept an uncomparable row.
**Recommendation: (a) drop it now, and if gemma matters, requeue at a matched budget with an n chosen
from a 5-item pilot.** A row that `compare` refuses is not evidence at any n.

### ~~O16~~ → CLOSED (operator, 2026-08-14): **LEAVE IT.** Deterministic, documented, 0.2% of items. Revisit only if a future axis leans on the affected instruction types.
### O16 (original text, retained). IFEval verifiers INVENT the criterion when a kwarg is absent — leave it, or stop grading those items?
Found and partly fixed 2026-08-14 (`b04030c`). 24 sites in the vendored `instructions.py` fabricate an
**absent kwarg** with `random.choice` / `random.randint` (e.g. `:1350`
`self._frequency = random.randint(1, _LETTER_FREQUENCY)`). Seeding made this **reproducible**; it did not
make it **valid** — for those items the grader checks the response against a threshold it made up.

**Measured scope: 1 verdict of 541 (0.2%), `acc` spread 0.18pp across 8 RNG states.** So this is currently
a hygiene question, not a live number — which is exactly when it is cheap to decide.

**Options:** (a) **leave it** — deterministic, documented, 0.2%; (b) **count those items as verifier
skips** so they leave the numerator AND are counted (the harness already counts skips visibly, so n
would not shrink invisibly) — costs a little n and is the most honest reading; (c) supply the upstream
IFEval defaults explicitly instead of drawing them, if upstream defines any.
**Recommendation: (a) leave it, with the note standing in the lab notebook.** At 0.2% the cure costs more
clarity than the disease, and (b) would make our `acc` non-comparable with published IFEval numbers.
**Revisit if a future axis leans on the affected instruction types** (`keywords:letter_frequency`,
`length_constraints:number_sentences`, and the other threshold-bearing ones).

### ~~O14~~ → CLOSED-BY-MEASUREMENT. The throughput concern DID NOT REPRODUCE; the rule stands
I reported a **>47× slowdown** at `262144/262144` (a matched item not completing in 19.5 min vs 24.8 s
at `131072/131072`) and wrote it into `AGENTS.md` as a challenge to the prealloc rule. **A 3-arm OFAT
falsifies it.** Same item (`HumanEval/146`), same model/sampling, router restarted per arm:

| cap / prealloc | wall | decode | peak mem | pressure warns |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 131072 / 131072 | 24.7 s | 98.5 tok/s | 25.35 GB | 0 |
| 262144 / 131072 | 25.0 s | 103.1 tok/s | 25.50 GB | 0 |
| **262144 / 262144** (shipped) | **27.8 s** | **104.5 tok/s** | 28.18 GB | 1 |

**All three are fast.** The shipped config is not slow, peak memory is far under the 46GB gate, and
the operator's rationale — prealloc is what makes 256K reachable at all, because growing 128K→256K
requires holding 384K of KV during the double-buffer — stands unchallenged.

**The original 19.5-minute non-completion is UNATTRIBUTED and treated as a transient.** Most likely
environmental: that run began with ~21GB already in use by other processes and swap already active
(router logged `System memory: 68.7GB total, 47.8GB available`, then
`memory.pressure.warn ram_percent=76.9`), against a cleaner box now.

**Process lesson, worth more than the result:** one dramatic observation, on ONE item, with TWO
variables moved at once, is a hypothesis. I wrote it up as a finding, put a warning into `AGENTS.md`
against a rule that prevents a known OOM, and reduced the registry on that basis. The OFAT cost 4
minutes and should have come first. (The registry change to 131072 is retained for an unrelated and
VALID reason — it is the tightest cap that keeps the declared 81,920 thinking budget in force with no
clamp — but the evalplus H2H therefore ran at a right-sized benchmark cap, not the shipped one, and
peak memory shows there was never a memory reason to reduce it.)

### ~~O13~~ → CLOSED (operator, 2026-08-14). My framing was WRONG; the config is self-consistent
I claimed `max_tokens: 102400` "exceeds the usable context" because at a 250K prompt the resolved
thinking budget collapses to 9,708. **Operator correction: 256K of context is a TOTAL budget — prompt
+ thinking + output — so a 250K prompt was never a supported configuration.**

Working it through with the shipped numbers: `thinking_budget` 81920 is exactly `0.8 × 102400`, and the
clamp yields the full budget while `0.8 × (262144 − prompt) ≥ 81920`, i.e. **prompt ≤ 159,744**. So
~160K is the DESIGNED maximum prompt, `max_tokens` and the 0.8 ratio agree, and **the clamp firing above
~160K is correct, intended behaviour rather than a defect.** Lowering `max_tokens` or `thinking_budget`
(my options 1 and 2) would have solved a non-problem at the cost of capability.

**What remains genuinely wrong is narrower — documentation and guard-rails, not the values:**
1. **The per-model max prompt is written down nowhere.** 159,744 for
   `Ornith-1.0-35B-mlx-uniform-4bit` is derivable but undocumented, and it is LOWER for
   `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit`, which wants a bigger thinking budget. Nobody can respect a
   limit they cannot look up. **→ record the derived max prompt per model in the registry.**
2. **The clamp is silent** while its sibling `max_tokens` clamp logs a warning. That silence is what let
   33 IFEval rows score as converged. **→ one-line fork fix to log it.**
3. **Benchmarks have no preflight against it.** A long-context axis feeding a 200K prompt would silently
   measure a shrunken thinking budget. **→ assert `prompt ≤ documented_max_prompt(model)`.** Unlike the
   `max_tokens ≤ cap` invariant I withheld, this one does NOT fail against the shipped config, so it can
   land honestly.

### ~~O12~~ → CLOSED (operator, 2026-08-14): **RE-RULED AS BUDGET HITS — these are NOT converged responses.**
M1 (2026-08-13) ruled the degenerate-loop rows "NOT a DNF... a valid converged response — converged as
determined by the MODEL", turning on item 2849 sitting at **"64% of budget"** (52,503 / 81,920) with
`finish_reason "stop"`.
**Against the budget actually in force that row is at 100.2%** — `int((65536 - 42) * 0.8) = 52,395`. It
is a budget hit. The model did not determine it was done; `ThinkingBudgetCriteria` force-injected
`</think>` and the model then wrote its answer, which is exactly why `finish_reason` read `"stop"`.
Reclassified: of 40 flagged loops, **25 + 8 are clamped budget hits, 4 + 2 are `max_tokens`
truncations, and 1 is genuinely self-terminating** (Ornith id 3748, 8,228 tokens).
**What is unchanged:** the answers still verify, so `acc` is unaffected (90.0% / 89.9%), and the IFEval
"the two winners are EQUIVALENT" headline survives — both arms moved the same way.
**What changes:** these rows are non-converged, so AGENTS.md's standing rule applies in full — a
persistent budget hit is a FAIL SIGNAL and the knob is temperature. `acc_strict` drops 89.8% → 86.7%
(Ornith) and 88.5% → 85.1% (distill).
**Recommendation: re-rule them as budget hits** (which the harness now does automatically, `aca967b`),
and keep the M1 *principle* — a genuinely self-terminating loop is not a DNF — since it was correct and
now applies to exactly one row. **This also un-blocks O9 in a smaller form:** the judge-panel question
about looped-but-correct answers now has an n of 1, so it is not worth panel time.

### ~~O11~~ → CLOSED-BY-MEASUREMENT. Premise falsified: the loops do not self-terminate
O11 asked whether to chase the degenerate-loop tax (42% / 57% of IFEval wall-clock) ahead of P4, on the
basis that these were "verbatim repetition loops that SELF-TERMINATE under budget and answer
CORRECTLY", i.e. a pure cost problem fixable by sampling.

**Measured 2026-08-14: 39 of the 40 rows are EXTERNAL TRUNCATIONS, not self-terminations.** The server
silently clamped the declared 81,920 thinking budget to ~52,390, and the harness compared against the
declared value — so a forced `</think>` scored as a clean stop. Genuinely self-terminating share of
wall-clock: **0.3% (Ornith) and 0.0% (distill)**, versus the 42% / 57% the item was raised on.

| | Ornith | distill |
|---|---|---|
| clamped budget-hits | 25 | 8 |
| `max_tokens` truncations | 4 | 2 |
| **genuinely self-terminating** | **1** | **0** |

**The cost is still real** (2.5h and 3.6h of wall-clock) — the *attribution* changed, and with it the
intervention. This is no longer "find the sampling setting that stops a self-verification loop"; it is
AGENTS.md's standing case: a model persistently hitting a generous budget → run the TEMPERATURE LADDER
on the offending items. That is a different experiment with a different success criterion, so O11 as
specified is answered rather than deferred. The design constraints attached to it (vary sampling on the
SAME loop-triggering ids; never the untruncated config; no `min_p` cells below temp 0.4) all still hold
for whatever replaces it. Successor questions: **O12** (the ruling), **O13** (the config), **O14** (the
prealloc rule).
⚠️ **Two blockers remain for any successor probe, both unbuilt:** `generate` has **no id filter**, so
"vary sampling on the SAME items" is not executable; and there is no `presence_penalty` /
`repetition_penalty` override (only `--max-tokens`, `--temp`, `--thinking-budget`). Both small, both
free. Also unverified: AGENTS.md claims a nonzero `presence_penalty` disables suffix decoding — I found
no such gate in the fork, only the structured-output one at `generation.py:1750`. Measure before
designing a penalty cell.

### ~~O11 (original text, retained)~~ Investigate the degenerate-loop tax BEFORE P4? (largest unclaimed speed win on the board)
Measured on IFEval 2026-08-13, and it is not a small effect:

| | `Ornith-1.0-35B-mlx-uniform-4bit` | `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` |
|---|---|---|
| loops | 30/541 (5.5% of items) | 10/148 (6.8%) |
| **share of wall-clock** | **42%** (2.5h of 6.0h) | **57%** (3.6h of 6.3h) |
| healthy → loop, mean wall | 25s → 303s (**12×**) | 71s → 1,283s (**18×**) |
| healthy → loop, mean tokens | 2,655 → 52,911 | 2,028 → 55,274 |
| **recoverable if fixed** | **2.3h of 6.0h (~38%)** | **3.4h of 6.3h (~54%)** |

`Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` is worse not because it loops more often (6.8% vs 5.5%) but
because it decodes at **28 tok/s against Ornith's 106**, so the same ~53K-token loop costs ~21 min
instead of ~5.

**For scale:** every Phase-2 speed lever combined was ~1.27× (suffix) plus ~2–7% (GQA kernel). This is
potentially **1.6–2.2× on real workloads** — larger than everything Phase 2 shipped, and the Tier-0
machinery to test it already exists.

**Mechanistic hint, which the test must respect:** loops concentrate on *counting* instructions
(`change_case:capital_word_frequency` **33%** vs a 4.5% base rate) — the model enumerates candidates to
verify its own output. That is the same pathology AGENTS.md records for the synthetic `aggregation`
word-tally task, now seen on a published benchmark. So it may be **prompt-triggered rather than purely a
sampling artifact**, and a temperature sweep alone cannot distinguish those. Design the probe to vary
sampling on the SAME loop-triggering items (the 30 + 10 ids are recorded in `degenerate_eosed_ids`).

⚠️ **Do not conflate with quality.** These answers are CORRECT — item 2849 passed both verifiers after
52,503 tokens of a two-line cycle. This is a COST question; the ruling that they are not DNFs stands (M1).

**Recommendation: yes, ahead of P4.** It is cheap, reuses existing tooling, targets the campaign's
stated speed goal, and P4's own cost estimate (~28h) is inflated by exactly this tax — so fixing it first
makes P4 cheaper too.


### ~~O10~~ → CLOSED (operator, 2026-08-14): **option 3.** `acc` keeps its historical meaning so published rows stay comparable; `acc_strict` carries the don't-grade-a-truncation semantics.
Raised by the operator's instruction to "call it DNF and **not grade it**" (2026-08-13). Half of that is
implemented, half is not:
- `converged: False` ✓ — the row IS marked, and a budget hit already fails the ratified formula.
- **but `acc` still includes it.** AGENTS.md fixes `acc` as "correctness over generated items
  (historical meaning)". The metric that zeroes truncated draws is `acc_strict` — and AGENTS.md
  explicitly forbids `acc_strict` as the ranking key, because it rises monotonically with
  `thinking_budget`, so a 5× budget spread would masquerade as capability.

So "don't grade it" collides with a ratified rule, and there are three coherent options:
1. **Status quo:** `acc` includes it, `acc_strict` zeroes it, both reported. (What AGENTS.md says now.)
2. **Drop from the denominator:** exclude non-converged rows from `acc` entirely. Cleanest reading of
   the operator's principle — but it makes `acc` non-comparable with every historical row, and a model
   that truncates often would be scored on a smaller, easier subset (the same bias that the nltk
   `punkt_tab` skip produced: 21% dropped moved acc 93.3% → 90.2%).
3. **Report `acc` over converged items only, as a NAMED metric** (`pass@1|converged` already exists and
   is computed), keeping `acc` historical.
**Recommendation: option 3.** It gives the operator's semantics an honest name without silently
redefining a metric that historical rows are published under, and it avoids option 2's
smaller-easier-subset bias. **NOTE:** no row in the IFEval run exceeded the thinking budget (0 of 220),
so this is currently a rule-hygiene question rather than one affecting a live number.


### ~~O1~~ → CLOSED, see C10
Not a design question so much as "may I fix these", but both mislead a reader on every run:
- **`grade.py` prints a retracted decision rule.** `CONV_GATE = 0.90` is still live and the scoreboard
  footer prints *"DECISION RULE (pre-registered): conv% >= 90 is a GATE; pass@1|conv RANKS within it."*
  AGENTS.md records that gate as **WITHDRAWN — unratified and unsound** (quantized in n; a point
  estimate against a hard threshold; ~10× too lenient on cost grounds). So every run prints a
  withdrawn rule while calling it pre-registered, and `conv_gate_pass` is still computed from it.
- **`run.py` mislabels its own sampling.** It prints `[params] using per-model production params
  (model_params.py)` immediately before `[generate] sampling profile = deployed`. The manifest proves
  `deployed` is what is used, so this is a stale log line — but "production" vs "deployed" is exactly
  the distinction that decides whether a run measured what we ship, so it is a dangerous thing to
  print wrongly.
**Recommendation:** fix both; replace the footer with the vector-reporting language AGENTS.md
actually ratifies. **Cost:** small, TDD-able, no model time.

## CLOSED-BY-MEASUREMENT

**The verdicts are here; the measured detail lives in `docs/lab-notebook.md`** (the APC work, M2/M3,
is written out at length there — byte-identical peak memory across arms, zero cache hits, and the
session-cache shadowing that explains it). Kept as verdicts rather than re-transcribed, because
"we decided" and "we measured" age differently and the measurement is the part that needs its
full context.

### M1. Should `converged` absorb self-terminating repetition loops? — **NO** (2026-08-13, operator-confirmed)
**Operator ruling: this is NOT a DNF. It is a valid converged response — converged as determined by the
MODEL — and it is recorded and evaluated for quality like any other row: correctness AND subjective
quality.**

The operator's principle — *exhausting the thinking budget is a failed run, because we interrupted the
model rather than it deciding it was done* — is correct **and already the implemented rule**:
`converged = finish_reason=="stop" AND completion_tokens < thinking_budget`, so a budget hit is already
not converged, and AGENTS.md already calls it a fail signal. (Terminology: **EOS = end-of-SEQUENCE**,
the model's own stop token — not end-of-session. My jargon caused the confusion.)

Item 2849 was **not** a budget exhaustion: 52,503 tokens at **64% of budget** with `finish_reason
"stop"`, i.e. the model stopped itself, and its answer **PASSED both strict and loose** verifiers
(`benchmark/m1/inspect_item.py`) — a valid 276-char answer. The work was complete by the model's own
decision, so marking it DNF would **discard a valid result**, the opposite error to the one the budget
rule guards against.

⚠️ **REFINEMENT (2026-08-13, correcting my own framing).** I called this "a COST defect, not a
correctness one". That is accurate but INCOMPLETE, and the incompleteness matters: what was actually
measured is only the cost half (271 s and 52,503 tokens for 276 chars). Whether a model that loops
2,071 times before answering produced *good work* is a question the instrumentation cannot answer, and
"correctness passed, so it is fine apart from the tokens" must not stand in for it. Per the campaign's
instrumentation rule, subjective quality belongs to the blind mixed-family JUDGE PANEL over
execution-PASSING outputs — and this row is exactly such an output, so it is **eligible, not exempt**.

**So `is_degenerate` is a FLAG FOR THE JUDGE PANEL'S ATTENTION, not a verdict.** Consequences:
- These rows stay in `acc` and in the item set. **No exclusion, no reweighting, no DNF.**
- `degenerate_wall_share` / `degenerate_token_share` remain reasoning-token COST, reported beside
  capability and never folded into it.
- The open question becomes **O9** below: does the panel rate looped-then-correct answers differently
  from directly-correct ones? Better question, and answerable.
- ⚠️ Caveat: the judge panel is on record as **"NOT RELIABLE ENOUGH TO RANK"** (v2, two runs). It can
  raise a signal here; it cannot settle it, and a panel result on this must not be presented as
  decisive.

### M2. How do APC and session caching compose? — **THEY DON'T** (2026-08-13)
Mutually exclusive per request: `generation.py:2455-2464` dispatches any request with a
`prompt_cache_state` to `_process_cached_request` and `continue`s past the BatchGenerator, the only
site passed `apc_manager`. Anonymous requests resolve to a session by chained message hashes, so all
of our traffic takes the session path and APC is never consulted (measured: 0 lookups, 0 stores, 0
resident bytes; peak memory bit-identical to APC-absent). Session caching is what makes multi-turn
cheap (measured: cost per NEW token flat, cost per TOTAL token −17×).

### M3. Does APC cost memory that could disqualify a candidate? — **NO** (2026-08-13)
It consumes nothing because it does nothing (see M2). Constraint satisfied, for the wrong reason.

| C26 | Are per-request seeds honored on the cached path? | 🛑 **ESCALATED 2026-08-26: C26 kills PAIRING for M23** — corrected same day (Fable review): the first escalation overclaimed "the A/B design cannot work"; the measured structure supports a narrower, still-serious conclusion. Measured: the SAME model (`Qwen3.8-27B-4bit`) on the SAME 20 humanevalplus items with the SAME declared seeds, two sessions (`m23` vs `m23b`) → **0/20 identical outputs**; 4 items diverged ≥2×; `HumanEval/146` went 7,106 → 82,168 completion tokens (11.6×), converged → budget-hit. **What this kills: per-item PAIRING.** Matched-seed cross-arm contrasts do not exist on this serving path, and the flagship M23 evidence dissolves ("`HumanEval/146` 279 s official vs ceiling on ours" is not a model difference — the official model itself produced 82,168 tokens on that item in another session). **What this does NOT kill: the unpaired A/B.** Cross-session structure is mostly per-item chaotic draws, not a coherent session shift: rank corr of log(completion_tokens) across sessions = **0.84** (item difficulty stable, divergence concentrated in a few tail items), same-model non-termination rate 1/20 → 2/20. Under that structure an unpaired comparison with item-level cluster bootstrap remains valid at ~√2 worse MDE; a session-level random effect is a RISK to bound (C30), not a demonstrated n=1 confound. **FIX LANDED + GATE RUN 2026-08-26 (fork `ab5708a5`, TDD, full suite 3617/0):** ar.py's `_PositionedTargetSampler` widened to the server's top_p/min_p/top_k filter chain and the two twins DE-DUPLICATED (server imports the ar class — one definition by construction); the seeded guard now excludes only top_n_sigma/p_less/typical_p. Verified live at the deployed profile (t0.6, top_k 20): same seed twice = byte-identical, seeds 11 vs 12 = different — the original repro inverted. **GATE RESULT: EQUAL BYTES across two full router/worker restarts** (seed 11: 268=268 tok; seed 12: 570=570 tok) → per the pre-registered rule the M23 redesign is the PAIRED m23c re-run, both arms pinned 5200 s. Caveats: probe = 2 seeds x 1 short prompt (<=600 tok); a 30k-token generation has more room for kernel-level float nondeterminism; the 2026-08-23 'cross-restart determinism NOT guaranteed' measurement predates the fix and is EXPLAINED by it (unseeded global RNG). NOTE the fix is fork-local: gate ran via PYTHONPATH override; the stack submodule bump waits on push approval — a runserver/router start from the submodule still runs the UNFIXED sampler until then. Original design-gate spec: one item, same seed, two sessions, byte-compare — equal bytes → paired M23 re-run; different bytes (possible even with honored seeds; cross-restart nondeterminism was measured 2026-08-23 and may be kernel-level) → unpaired with session replicates (≥2 sessions/arm, ABBA order). **ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-08-26 (code read, fork `../mlx-vlm`)**: the cached path passes no `sampler=`, so `generate/ar.py:generate_step` builds one — and its seeded branch is gated on `min_p == DEFAULT_MIN_P AND top_k == DEFAULT_TOP_K (=0) AND no top_n_sigma/p_less/typical_p`. Every deployed profile sets `top_k: 20`, so the guard ALWAYS fails on our traffic and generation falls into unseeded `make_sampler(...)` → the request seed is silently dropped. (The batched path is immune: the server builds its own extended `_PositionedTargetSampler` — which DOES support top_k/min_p — and threads `seeds=[request_seed]` through `BatchGenerator.insert`.) The guard exists because `ar.py`'s `_PositionedTargetSampler` twin is the OLD narrow one (temperature/top_p/seed only) — two drifted copies of the same class. Fix shape: port top_k/min_p parity into `ar.py`'s sampler (or de-duplicate the twins), widen the guard, and verify the decode loop routes through the KEYED draw (`sample_target`; note the class's plain `__call__` ignores keys entirely — a second latent seed-drop). TDD; fork tests touch Metal, so they run only after the m23b arm exits. <!-- allow-shorthand --> Original entry: **NO — BUG CONFIRMED** (2026-08-25: seeds 11 vs 12 byte-identical at t=1.0, same session, cached path). Single-sample runs unaffected; multi-sample cached-path runs made k copies — audit those; fork fix queued. **Operator ruling 2026-08-25: the fork fix is funded AHEAD of M24** (a silent seed bug poisons any future multi-sample cached-path run; M24 absorbs the delay). Implementation starts after the M23 arms complete (no fork test runs against the box mid-measurement). Original entry: Probe side-observation: seeds 11/12/13 gave byte-length-identical completions in both arms (459/459/459 and 421/421/421). Could be near-deterministic t=0.3 on a trivial prompt, or `seed` ignored inline — the latter breaks paired draws for M6b (k samples = k copies). Cheap check: two seeded requests on the cached path, compare BYTES; zero model-time beyond seconds | 2026-08-24 |

| C28 | The 3600s `--probe-timeout` default is below the derived floor, and abandoned generations starve the NEXT item | **DEFECT CONFIRMED, mechanism verified 3/3** (2026-08-25, M23 official arm). Two coupled problems. (a) `run.py --probe-timeout` defaults to a HARDCODED 3600s, not derived: at this arm's floor decode rate (22.3 tok/s) a legitimate full-budget draw needs 102400/22.3 = 4592s + prefill + headroom (~5200s), so budget-exhausting items are censored as `error_kind=probe_timeout` carrying NO `completion_tokens` — the loop taxonomy for exactly the items that most need it is destroyed. (b) The client abandons at 3600s but **the worker does not cancel the orphan**, so the next item queues behind it: every one of the 3 timeouts is followed by exactly one starved neighbour and NO other row shows the pattern (`HumanEval/2`->`HumanEval/82` gap 573s; `HumanEval/132`->`HumanEval/7` gap 685s; `Mbpp/440`->`Mbpp/139` gap 2051s; median gap over all other rows 0.4-0.6s). Starved rows' CONTENT is valid (normal 26-27 tok/s decode, sane token counts, finish=stop) — only their `wall_s` is contaminated, so they must be EXCLUDED from latency-per-task while staying in accuracy. Orphan total runtimes (3600 + neighbour gap = 4173s/4285s/5651s) are consistent with all three running to the max_tokens ceiling at a context-declining rate, i.e. they are genuine runaways and DNF is substantively the right classification (O35's rule stands). **NOT changed for M23**: the 3600s bound is applied identically to both arms, so the A/B stays apples-to-apples; raising it mid-pairing would vary a second param. ⚠️ **SEVERITY UPGRADED 2026-08-26 — this is not a reporting nuisance, it INVALIDATES RUNS.** The abandoned generation does not merely bill its successor: it CASCADES. On the M23 `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` arm, one runaway's orphan starved item 34, which timed out and added a second orphan, and so on — items 34-39 were a perfectly consecutive block of six false DNFs, and `grade`'s own guard failed the run (HARNESS-BROKEN, 35 % > 20 % error rows). Proven by controlled single-item re-runs (unload → VERIFY zero workers → load → ONE item): `Mbpp/803` DNF'd at 3600 s in-arm and CONVERGED IN 107.2 s clean, while `HumanEval/146` DNF'd at 1800 s clean and is a genuine runaway. **So a single long generation can convert an unbounded number of SUBSEQUENT items into false DNFs, and the resulting rate is uninterpretable.** Note this also means killing a driver does NOT stop the work — a killed run's orphan contaminated my first diagnostic. **The P19 prioritisation was WRONG in one respect and RIGHT in another**: cancel-on-disconnect is not mere defence-in-depth (it is what makes an interrupted run safe to follow), but the DERIVED BOUND remains the primary fix and now solves BOTH problems, because a bound above full-budget generation time means nothing is ever abandoned in normal operation, so no orphan is ever created. **OPERATOR RULINGS 2026-08-25**: (1) **DERIVE THE DEFAULT — funded, quality-maximizing, bundled into the C26 fork/harness session.** It fixes the information DESTRUCTION at source (a budget-hit becomes a real row: `finish_reason=length`, `completion_tokens=budget`, so the convergence vector and loop taxonomy survive) and as a side effect largely eliminates orphans, since a bound above the legitimate maximum means well-behaved draws are never abandoned. **Rider, mandatory: `probe_timeout` becomes OUTPUT-DETERMINING for DNF classification once it can vary, so it MUST enter the provenance fingerprint and `compare` must REFUSE across differing bounds** — otherwise 3600s rows and 5400s rows pool with incompatible DNF semantics. (2) **Cancel-on-disconnect stays queued separately as defense-in-depth** — it fixes only the collateral (neighbour latency + wasted box time) and cannot recover a single lost token count, so it is subordinate to (1). (3) **Censored-runaway diagnostic APPROVED**: after both M23 arms are generated AND graded, re-run the timed-out ids from BOTH arms under a separate tune label at a derived (~5400s) bound, strictly OUT of the A/B pool, to recover the loop taxonomy symmetrically | 2026-08-25 |
| C29 | `compare()` CANNOT compute a paired `acc_strict` delta — it silently drops DNFs | **FIXED 2026-08-25, TDD, suite 1199/0. NO EXISTING RESULT INVALIDATED** — audited before fixing: `cmd_compare` never passes `metric`, so every comparison ever run used the default `acc`, whose generated-only meaning is BY DESIGN (`grade.py`: "`acc` keeps its generated-only meaning"); the string `metric="acc_strict"` appears nowhere in the codebase, so the broken path was never invoked; per-arm `acc_strict` scalars come from `grade` and were always correct; and the fix can only change a comparison where DNFs exist — M6b's arms carry 0 errors in both legs (63/63), so its +1.6pp CI [0.0,+4.8] stands unchanged. Fix: `grade` now EXPORTS the strict vector as `score["strict_items"]` (in-memory only, stripped on persistence via the new `_IN_MEMORY_ONLY`, so score.json shape is unchanged) and `compare` selects that vector when `metric="acc_strict"`, REFUSING when it is absent rather than falling back to the graded-only vector — a silent fallback fails in the direction that looks like "no difference". Deriving strict twice is what let the two drift, so `grade` now owns the semantics and `compare` only chooses the vector. Regression test asserts a half-DNF arm scores 0.5 vs 1.0 strict while pairing over all 4 items. Original entry (defect, found by code read while preparing M23 scoring): `compare()` loads scores via `grade.grade()` and takes per-item vectors from `_per_item(score)`, which reads `score["items"]` — the GRADED list, i.e. generated rows only (`compare.py:317-320`, `_per_item` at :142). `grade` builds the strict vector separately (`grade.py:169-179`: `strict_items` = graded scores with non-converged forced to 0.0, PLUS every errored row appended at score 0.0) and uses it ONLY for the scalar `score["acc_strict"]` — it is never written back into `score["items"]`. `compare.py` contains ZERO occurrences of "strict": the `metric` argument only selects REFUSAL rules (`_HARDWARE_METRICS`, `peak_mem_gb`, `conv_only = metric=="pass_at_1_converged"`), it does not change which vectors are paired. **Consequence: passing `metric="acc_strict"` returns a delta computed over the generated-only intersection, excluding exactly the DNF items.** Harmless when DNF rates are low and symmetric (every axis so far), CATASTROPHIC on a DNF-asymmetric axis: on M23 the effect IS the DNF asymmetry (~25-33% for `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` vs 3% for `Qwen3.8-27B-4bit`), so `compare` would have reported a near-zero delta on the survivors and inverted the verdict — while looking authoritative, since it comes from the campaign's own guarded tool. **Per-arm `acc_strict` scalars from `grade` are CORRECT and unaffected.** M23 workaround (no harness change mid-axis): build strict per-item vectors directly (graded score, non-converged -> 0.0, DNF rows -> 0.0), restrict to the shared ids, and pass them to `stats.cluster_bootstrap` — the sanctioned estimator — rather than to `compare()`. Asks: (1) give `compare` a real strict path that injects DNFs, and (2) make it REFUSE `metric="acc_strict"` until then, so the trap cannot be stepped on silently | 2026-08-25 |
| C30 | How much of any single-session arm's result is SESSION NOISE? | **OPEN (2026-08-26, raised by the session review).** The m23/m23b replicate (same model `Qwen3.8-27B-4bit`, same 20 humanevalplus items, two sessions) gives the first estimate free: 0/20 byte-identical, log-token rank corr 0.84, non-termination 1/20 vs 2/20, 4/19 items ≥2× token divergence with `HumanEval/146` swinging converged→budget-hit. Why it matters beyond M23: EVERY certified tune and pick comparison in the campaign was measured in one session per arm; if tail items flip converged↔budget-hit across sessions, `acc_strict` on a 20–50-item arm carries session noise of order 1–2 items (±2–5 pp) that no current interval accounts for. **mbppplus half EXTRACTED 2026-08-26 (arm complete): session variance is WORSE than the humanevalplus half** — 1/20 byte-identical, log-token rank corr 0.666 (vs 0.84), 11/20 items with ≥2× token divergence (`Mbpp/300` 1,176→29,992 = 25×; `Mbpp/232` 6,407→318 = 20×; `Mbpp/785` 12×), and `Mbpp/306` swung 33,304→82,330 tokens — converged→budget-hit across sessions with finish==stop BOTH times (the false-pass shape). Caveat: the m23 side ran at the pre-C28 3600s bound vs m23b's pinned 5200s — no shared item errored on either side, so bytes/tokens compare cleanly, but wall times do not. Reading: reasoning-heavier corpora amplify the chaotic tail redraw; a 20-item mbppplus arm carries ±1–2 items of pure session noise in acc_strict. **GOLD-STANDARD REPLICATES UNLOCKED 2026-08-26 evening (M23 identity discovery)**: the m23/m23b/m23c "arms" of `Qwen3.8-27B-4bit` and `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` are the SAME MODEL (2179/2180 tensors md5-identical), so the corpus already holds FIVE sessions of one model over overlapping items — including the unseeded-era cross-"arm" gaps (acc up to 6.6pp, 10× token divergence on `Mbpp/803`, DNF asymmetry) which are now pure session-noise measurements, and the seeded m23c session (hep 100% strict conv 100%, mbpp 80%/80% conv 100%, ZERO runaways vs m23b's 2 budget-hits — same model, same items; whether honored seeds systematically avoid runaway trajectories is an open sub-question, n=1 session). Any future re-grade can add replicate rows. Deliverable: a session-variance bound to quote alongside MDE, and a rule for when an axis needs session replicates. **BOUND DELIVERED 2026-08-29 (M26 replicates — the deliverable exists)**: six same-model s1→s2 opencode pairs (3 models × python+javascript, 22 items each, per-row protocol parity verified) measure per-item cross-session discordance q = **0.114** `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit`, **0.227** `Ornith-1.0-35B-mlx-uniform-4bit`, **0.432** `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` (pooled 34/132 = 0.26). Quotable bound: 1-sd session noise on a single-session 22-item agentic arm = √(22·q/2) ≈ **1.1 / 1.6 / 2.2 items (5 / 7 / 10 pp)** per model respectively; observed 2-sd envelope reaches ±6 (the pick's python 12→18). Session noise is a MODEL PROPERTY (4× spread across models measured under one protocol), so it must be quoted per-model, and net deltas understate it — use flip counts (up to 9+3 per pair). RULE (proposed, applied prospectively): every single-session agentic arm quotes q alongside the MDE; an axis needs ≥2 sessions/arm whenever it informs a pick/certification decision or the expected discordant margin is < 2× the combined session sd (≈3 items at n=22 for these models); single-session arms may claim DIRECTION only. Agentic-arm churn is far above the text-bench replicate estimate (±1–2 items/20) — gate-adjacent items (stall/loop boundary) are the flip mass | 2026-08-26 |

| C34 | Scoresheet shadowing defect: tune-suffixed evalplus `*_samples.jsonl` sidecars hide graded cells | **FIXED (operator-approved + landed 2026-08-26, TDD: failing test watched fail, then the one-line guard change; scoreboard tests 20/20).** Re-emit surfaced 15 shadowed graded cells (both winners' n=100 humanevalplus/mbppplus among them); spliced into `docs/campaign-results.md` with two NEW selection caveats recorded there (largest-n surfaces the quarantined `t0.5prod` arms for the two distills, and the INVALIDATED m23 arms for `Qwen3.8-27B-4bit` — the presenter has no row-quality concept; changing selection would need its own proposal). Original entry: **OPEN — fix proposed, needs approval (2026-08-26).** Root cause found: `scoreboard.collect()` skips samples sidecars by testing `bench.endswith("_samples")` where `bench = f.stem.split(".")[0]` — that catches `humanevalplus_samples.jsonl` but NOT tune-suffixed `humanevalplus.m23_samples.jsonl` (bench parses as `humanevalplus`). The sidecars are padded full-corpus evalplus sample files (164/378 rows of bare `task_id`+`solution`, no score.json), so they win the largest-n variant selection and print `164/378 ungraded`, shadowing genuinely graded smaller variants (e.g. `Qwen3.8-27B-mlx-uniform-4bit` humanevalplus t0.6 n=47 acc 89.4/strict 84.0, and all m23c grades). Confirmed: the 2026-08-26 re-emit is byte-identical to the last splice — the m23c grades are invisible. Proposed fix (one line + TDD failing test first): change the skip to `f.stem.endswith("_samples")` (the stem keeps the tune suffix, so it catches both forms). No graded number changes; only shadowed cells surface. Re-emit + splice after approval | 2026-08-26 |
| C35 | Bench provenance records the REGISTRY's `draft_kind`, not the SERVED one — bench runs of a draft-certified pick fingerprint as `mtp` while verifiably serving draft-OFF | **FIXED (operator-approved + landed 2026-08-26 night, TDD: 7 tests watched fail first; bench suite 1230/0).** (a) `paths.registry_path()` honors `MLX_SERVE_CONFIG` (absolute or repo-root-relative), so a driver launched with the overlay fingerprints the served config; (b) `registry_draft()` tripwire: when a live worker's `--model` carries the entry's hf_path, its cmdline draft flags are cross-checked against the yaml — observed mismatch RAISES (refuses the run), match stamps `draft_source: registry+worker`; a worker for another model or no worker leaves the yaml answer untouched, and model-not-in-registry keeps its `unknown` wildcard. Disposition executed: the poisoned pilot rows (`humanevalplus.d64k` jsonl+manifest under the pick) DELETED after their sizing was recorded in PLAN M12; the red provenance test recovered. **Operational rule from here: bench DRIVERS launch with `MLX_SERVE_CONFIG` pointed at the draft-stripped overlay, same as the router.** Original entry: **OPEN — fix proposal awaits ruling (2026-08-26 evening).** Found by `test_a_v3_current_does_NOT_condemn_the_REAL_corpus_on_disk` going red on the M12 pilot's fresh `humanevalplus.d64k.manifest.json`: `runtime.draft_kind: mtp, draft_source: registry`. Mechanism: `provenance.registry_draft()` reads `paths.registry_path()`, which is HARD-WIRED to the repo `main_models.yaml` — but bench routers deliberately serve from the draft-stripped overlay (the M6b standing rule), so the first bench run of `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` since its `draft_kind: mtp` certification (`ca4ed0f`, 2026-08-25) recorded FALSE draft provenance. The worker cmdline was verified draft-free pre-launch, so the GENERATIONS are clean; only the manifest lies. This is the yaml-vs-worker trap (AGENTS.md: verify at the worker, never the yaml) reproduced INSIDE the instrument. Blast radius: any future bench run of any pick that ships a certified predictor; `compare` would refuse these rows against honest draft-OFF rows and would happily pool them with genuinely mtp-ON rows. The bench suite stays red (1222/1) until resolved — the failing test is a true positive, do not adjust it. Proposed fix (needs approval): (a) `paths.registry_path()` honors `MLX_SERVE_CONFIG` when set, so a driver launched with the overlay fingerprints the config actually served (also makes the `deployed` profile read the served registry — same generation_defaults in the overlay, and measure-what-you-serve is the correct semantics); (b) fail-loud tripwire: when the worker cmdline is observable, `provenance` cross-checks its draft flags against the yaml answer and REFUSES the run on mismatch instead of recording either. TDD both. Pilot-row disposition (also owed): rows are draft-OFF in reality but mtp-stamped — proposal: keep the pilot for SIZING only (its wall/token data is what the pilot exists for), never grade/pool it, and let the post-fix full run regenerate d64k rows under honest provenance (`--clean-stale` will condemn them anyway once the fingerprint reads `off`) | 2026-08-26 |

| C47 | `compare`'s code-sha guard keys on the WHOLE `src/mlx-vlm` submodule sha, so every fork commit — including tool-only ones like C41/C45 (MTP splitter, never imported by the server) — refuses pairing with every earlier row. Bit twice on 2026-09-02: the M21 reference had to be re-measured after a serving-relevant gap of 12 days (legitimate), and the C45 bump landed mid-ladder, forcing the worktree to be pinned back to `57177a21` so t0.7 pairs with t0.5/t0.6 (the venv imports `src/mlx-vlm` editable, so the pin is also what the next worker loads). Proposal: fingerprint a SERVING-PATH sha (git tree hash of `mlx_vlm/{server,generate,models,speculative/runtime}` + `src/mlx-serve`) instead of the commit sha, so tool/test-only commits do not orphan the corpus; keep the refusal semantics otherwise. Needs a ruling because it redefines which historical rows are pairable. Options: (a) add Claude Opus 5 as a third judge on all 820 packets, restoring the designed three-judge majority (~13M more tokens, ~2 h in waves of ten); (b) pre-registration amendment for a two-judge panel — a single judge's "tie" does not veto the other's verdict on anchors (post-hoc this run would pass 10/10, flagged as a post-hoc amendment); (c) accept the FAIL — no panel ranking this round, C ladder stays exactly as ruled in C67. Session recommendation: run M39 (mechanical vision smoke, GPU only, no judge tokens) FIRST — if vision quality already separates the seeing C contenders, the panel ranking matters less and (c) is fine; otherwise (a). **RULED (a)-staged 2026-09-12 (P336)**: Opus judges the 60 anchor packets first; three-judge gate over anchors PASSES on every metric (`degrade_accuracy` 1.00; `benchmark/results/judge_c_v1/gate.anchors3.json`; `docs/campaign-results.md` 2026-09-12). Stage 2 (Opus on the 760 item packets, ~10M tokens) awaits an explicit operator go; ranking withheld until then. **Stage 2 DONE 2026-09-12 (operator "go")**: Opus judged all 760 item packets; three-judge gate PASS; ranking reported (`docs/campaign-results.md` 2026-09-12); C reorder proposal filed as C69. | **DONE** | 2026-09-12 | <!-- allow-shorthand -->

| C97 | **Shipped web-search engine and configuration** (supersedes the C96 recommendation). Keyless is a ship requirement; only `searxng` and `duckduckgo` qualify, and the keyless Exa endpoint is MCP-only so OpenWebUI's `exa` provider cannot use it. Screened every keyless candidate alone (25 queries): `duckduckgo web`, `google`, `google cse`, `startpage` all 25/25 useful; `brave` 429s after ~6 queries then suspends 180 s; `duckduckgo` CAPTCHAs on every query; `mojeek` 403; `mwmbl` 3/25; `yep` 17/25. Shipped a THREE-engine pool (`duckduckgo web`, `startpage`, `google`) rather than the single-engine `keep_only` C96 proposed: measured 25/25 sequential and 8/8 burst on the real production request shape, and a one-engine config returns zero the moment its engine trips, whereas SearXNG's per-engine 180 s suspension routes around a failure automatically. Also removed four dictionary/translation/currency engines that were polluting `general`, fixed a latent `karmasearch` stub that broke engine registration, and closed the open-proxy exposure. [Evidence](websearch-searxng-qualification-2026-09-16.md). **Operator decisions still needed**: (a) `rag.top_k` 3 -> 12 is a GLOBAL RAG change affecting document chat, reasoned but unmeasured; (b) no end-to-end chat turn has been measured, so the `<source>` block the model receives is still unverified; (c) whether to pursue the deferred local index ([design](websearch-local-index.md)). | **RESOLVED 2026-09-20 with corrections** — pool ratified (operator, P1); (b) measured: 10/10 e2e gate on the NATIVE tool path (`websearch-e2e-gate-2026-09-20.md`); (a) moot — shipped models use native FC where `rag.top_k` is not on the path, AND the seed-file value never went live (C98); (c) stays deferred. | 2026-09-16 |

Needs an operator go; touches the C85-certified rewind path. **2026-09-27: periodic-snapshot design REJECTED in cold review; (b) narrowed to prompt-end retention = M48 (`docs/specs/c102b-prompt-end-retention.md`), operator go given; upstream sync prerequisite (M49) DONE 2026-09-28; **(b) DONE 2026-09-28 as B1+B2 together (operator P56.2): fork `1bd249d3`, four Codex rounds, live A2/A4/A5 PASS, A6 bounded PASS, A3 open → C105. Options: (a) opencode-side — exclude the synced dir from skill discovery (config) or prune stale copies; zero worker risk; (b) worker-side — periodic DeltaNet snapshots (C102(b) revised) so a mid-prefix divergence reuses everything before it; (c) both. | C107 | **M54 AgentBench OS design choices** (P2, 2026-09-30; spec `docs/specs/m54-agentbench-os.md`). **D1 agent harness**: (a) our `bench.agent_loop.run_agent` with upstream tools/prompt and `docker exec` per command — upstream-faithful, M50-guarded, outcomes labelled; (b) opencode as the agent — matches the shipped B harness but its bash tool runs on the host, so the task container is reachable only through a wrapper and the upstream protocol is lost. Recommend (a). **D2 exclusion rule**: quanteval's 30-item list is unpublished. Recommend a MECHANICAL rule: before any model call, run every task's `evaluation.example.code` in a fresh container; a task whose reference produces no gold answer or a different answer on two runs is excluded and listed in the corpus manifest; everything else stays, including under-specified prompts (a model failing them is still a result). **D3 predictor state**: this is a new cross-model axis → C71 says SELECTION is predictor-OFF; the picks ship ON. Recommend OFF for the two-arm comparison, with an ON certification arm on the leading pick only if the axis is later cited for a pick. **D4 round limit**: upstream 8. Keep 8 so results are interpretable against the published numbers; report `turn_cap` share so a tighter-than-needed cap is visible. | **RULED 2026-09-30 (operator: "Proceed as planned"): D1 (a) own loop, D2 mechanical reference-gold exclusion, D3 predictor OFF for the two-arm comparison, D4 round limit 8.** Build starts under M54; pre-registered acceptance criteria in the spec. **Amendment 2026-09-30 (cold review F4a, architect call, flagged for the operator):** D1's "`docker exec` per command" wording described an implementation detail, not an intent to diverge; the spec's "upstream-faithful protocol" governs, so each task gets ONE persistent `bash --login` shell inside its container (upstream `task.py`/AgentRL `create_shell`), because four `os-std` tasks depend on `start`-script shell state (`cd`, variables, `su`) and per-command exec made two of them unsolvable and one trivial. Other fidelity gaps closed the same round: re-prompt on a tool-less turn, 800-char output clipping with the upstream wrapper, first tool call only, 30 s exec timeout, upstream container limits (`/root`, 1 GiB, 2 vCPU). Loop guard disabled for this axis (the 8-round cap is the bound; repeats are counted). **Second amendment (cold review R3, architect call):** no fixed episode deadline — the build had introduced a 3600 s cap that would bind on every turn for both picks (derived per-turn timeouts ≈ 5300 s / 4800 s) and penalise the slower decoder; the deadline is 8 × the derived per-turn timeout, sized from the pilot if it ever needs tightening, never tuned. **Manual exclusion:** `std-007-84` (reference reads `$1`; the placeholder probe cannot catch numeric-id answers). **Result 2026-10-01 (chain 1, `b43c8e5`):** dense Qwen checkpoints tie at 0.61–0.62, `Ornith-1.0-35B-mlx-uniform-4bit` 0.549, `NVIDIA-Nemotron-3.5-Lightning-30B-A3B-4bit` 0.415; no ladder change proposed. **Third amendment (fidelity, rounds 13–19):** upstream AgentRL creates the shell with `tty=True`; the harness now uses a pty shell, bash-emitted `PROMPT_COMMAND` marker, whole-script rounds, upstream escape stripping, bashrc-installed marker (`df3b65c`). **Record 2026-10-02 (chain 3):** `Qwen3.6-27B-Opus-Distill-OptiQ-4bit` 0.620 (2 runaways), both picks 0.592, `Ornith-1.0-35B-mlx-uniform-4bit` 0.535, `NVIDIA-Nemotron-3.5-Lightning-30B-A3B-4bit` 0.423; no ladder change. **CLOSED.** | 2026-09-30 |
| C108 | **Native16 first pick + retained KV sessions leave ~10 GB headroom on the 64 GB box with docker running.** Measured 2026-10-01 during M54: each retained session preallocates the full 262144-token cap (~10 GB in native16 for `Qwen3.8-27B-Fable-Distill-OptiQ-4.5bpw-mixed`, ~2.5 GB for the TQ4 picks); with `MLX_VLM_CACHE_SESSION_MAX=2` a third (new) session pushed the box to 96–97 % and the router returned 500 (four times on one task, deterministic until the model was unloaded to drop the sessions; chain 1 saw it once). The TQ4 models never warned. The prealloc-equals-cap rule stands (operator 2026-08-14), so the levers are: (a) `MLX_VLM_CACHE_SESSION_MAX=1` for the native16 pick (benchmark drivers already use 2; daily driver uses the fork default unless set); (b) keep native16 and accept the headroom; (c) revisit TQ4 for the first pick (C81 is PROVISIONAL). Recommend (a) for benchmark routers now and a measured decision for the daily driver. **Operator P6 (2026-10-01):** session max 1 only limits retention and makes every client switch repay prefill — not acceptable for the daily driver. **Proposal:** `MLX_VLM_CACHE_SESSION_MAX=1` for LEAN BENCHMARK routers only (each task is one conversation; its turns reuse the active session, retained ones buy nothing there); daily driver stays at 2 with `cache_session_shrink` (C85) idle compaction, which benchmark back-to-back traffic never triggers. Open measurement: the idle-shrink delay vs typical client-switch gaps on the daily driver. The fork has NO pressure-based session eviction (LRU by count only); a pressure-aware eviction is a reasonable fork follow-up. | **RULED 2026-10-01 (operator): `MLX_VLM_CACHE_SESSION_MAX=1` for lean benchmark routers, `=2` for the daily driver.** AGENTS.md router line updated; `runserver.sh` already sets 2. **Follow-up CLOSED 2026-10-02 (operator P13): no pressure-based eviction in the fork — previously decided against as less reliable and not better overall.** | 2026-10-01 |
